[RESHARE] 2022-10-08 | activity 6984586288859406336 | likes 4545 | comments 273 | shares 182
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:6984586288859406336
(Reshared from: Dave Gerhardt)

I went from Marketing Manager to CMO in 4 years.

And damn did I make a bunch of mistakes along the way.

I didn't realize how different the role of "good marketer" was from the role of "good marketing leader."

After some time now away from a marketing leadership role (I'm retired from being a marketing leader, don't worry) I've had some time to think back on the things I wish I knew then.

Here they are:

16 Lessons I Wish I Knew As First Time B2B Marketing Leader

- Forget the acronyms & best practices, use your brain. Ultimately there are only three ways to grow a business: get more new customers, increase the transaction value, increase how often people buy from you. You can get lost in the MQL PQL SQL SAL definitions and best practices. Get back to the basics and operate from first principles.

- You need a story to be successful in B2B. Call it a category, call it a movement. Regardless, you need a story that people can bring to their boss about why they should buy your product beyond features + benefits. HubSpot created inbound marketing. Gainsight championed customer success. Drift conversational marketing. Each led through education + expertise and showed people a new way of doing things. Sell the vision and the story.

- You need to sweat the "offer" - request a demo and contact sales are only going to convert for the highest intent prospects. You need other offers. HubSpot's website grader. Drift did a "test drive" to test the chatbot on your website before talking to anyone. Demandwell has a free SEO audit. Clari has a revenue leakage calculator. These are all smart ideas that are still further down the funnel than ebook but work to get qualified buyers in to eventually talk to sales.

- Avoid incremental thinking. You can waste so many cycles thinking that the next homepage variation or the email series is going to be the thing that gets you out of the revenue hole. Rarely does "nurturing" solve the problem. Take bigger swings.

- You must find a way to get on the same page as sales, no matter what. Find a way to put all the internal politics aside. You will never be successful unless you can marketing sales + marketing = revenue *together*. Find out how the VP of sales is comped and do everything in your power to get on the same page. Health teams gives bonuses based on company goals not team goals. Find out and fix it. Manage up to the CEO if needed. Life is 100% easier when sales + marketing have shared revenue goals.

- Make finance and ops your best friends. YOU HAVE PLENTY TO DO ON YOUR OWN, YOUR JOB IS NOT FINANCE AND OPS I PROMISE YOU!!!! But instead find a way to make them your key business partners. Same way you need to be on the same team as sales, you need to be on the same team with finance and ops. Make them your best friends - collaborate on marketing as a business function Don’t be like 28 year old Dave “I got this myself” - DON’T GET DEFENSIVE - take the help, work together.

(continued in the comments)

[RESHARE] 2022-10-12 | activity 6986042481553420289 | likes 1129 | comments 230 | shares 83
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:6986042481553420289
(Reshared from: Chris Walker)

B2B companies are cutting Google paid search spend by 75% and seeing no negative impact on pipeline or revenue. Here's why:

When the economy was rolling the past few years, companies would drop hundreds of thousands of dollars per month on Google Ads to pump up vanity metrics for investors….or they just didn't look at it in enough detail to realize what was actually happening.

But now that there's pressure to deliver sustainable, profitable growth. This channel and large variable spend are finally getting scrutinized against actual business results. Not sign up form fills or "leads". 
 
So here’s what happens:
 
1. Bad Settings Get Turned on by Default - Every time you build a Google campaign (paid search, YouTube, Display), Google turns on certain parameters by default. These settings include Google Search Partner Network (paid search) and/or Audience Expansion (YouTube, Display). 
 
2. Set Your Conversion Objective - B2B advertisers will choose a conversion objective for the campaign, usually a Free Trial Sign Up (PLG) or Request a Demo (Enterprise Motion). 
 
3. Google Ads Drive High Volume Spam into the Desired Conversion Objective - Within 24 hours, you will receive SPAM // GARBAGE submissions into your form. 
 
Job title = Janitor, Plumber, Stripper, etc. 
Company = Fake companies or they say they work at a company like Tik Tok and they don’t
Email = All gmail, yahoo, or other free ESP emails 
 
Complete SPAM. 
 
For paid search, normally these have direct attribution in a marketing automation platform. So if advertisers wanted to see it, they could. But most never look.
 
For YouTube & Display, these leads are typically attributed to Organic/Direct and are counted as a “view-through conversions”. Most B2B Advertisers & MOPs professionals don’t see what’s actually happening. 
 
Media agencies that work with enterprise brands aren't in the CRM & never monitor the quality of the form submissions, so they sit back and celebrate because they got thousands of $6 "leads" from Google this month.

Many B2B Marketers are measured on volume of “leads” & cost per lead. They are just trying to get as many form fills as possible, regardless of quality. The only way to hit those targets are to do stuff like this that is 100% misaligned with business goals.

PLG companies (knowingly or unknowingly, I’m honestly not sure) run this method & pump up sign up numbers for when they go to investors to raise a Series B.

Thousands or tens of thousands of free trial sign up form submissions every month, none of them convert to paying users because it’s completely SPAM, but it looks good on an investor slide while the company wastes $100k to $1MM per month on these ads.

__

Anyone who is spending on Google Ads should review this in their instance ASAP. It will probably save you $10k/month minimum, hundreds of thousands for some brands :)

There’s something rotten going on in Google Advertising in B2B and I don’t like it.

#sales #marketing #b2b #google #demand

[RESHARE] 2022-10-14 | activity 6986528252576108544 | likes 626 | comments 69 | shares 37
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:6986528252576108544
(Reshared from: Chris Walker)

How B2B companies measure the success of their Marketing by design creates misalignment. Here’s why:
 
1. Wrong Marketing Goals 
 
Companies continue to score their Marketing team on “leads”, MQLs, MQAs, SQLs - all metrics that do not align with Sales outcomes or actual business results. The first step to fixing this is CHANGE THE GOAL. 

When you change the goal from “leads” to HIRO PIPELINE (pipeline that Sales wins at > 25%) and REVENUE, immediately the mindset needs to change. 
 
Because all the things Marketing does to hit their fluffy “lead” or MQL or SQL target, no longer get them to their goal. 
 
The way companies measure the success of the Marketing and Sales by design creates misalignment. This is a problem at the executive level and that’s it - there’s nothing else to say - it's up to the C-Level to solve this once and for all.
 
2. Not respecting the difference between CREATING DEMAND and CAPTURING DEMAND
 
When you look at these two core parts of a revenue-generating system independently, what you’ll realize is that B2B companies build their entire strategy and attribution system around capturing demand. 
 
Then, when you consider that Sales and SDRs are capture demand functions, companies will realize that > 95% of their commercial (marketing + sales) budget is spent on capturing demand. 
 
Their entire revenue-generating strategy is grossly overweight to capturing demand simply because of how they measure success. 
 
Smart companies are realizing they need to measure CAPTURING DEMAND and CREATING DEMAND separately. 
 
3. No attribution strategy to effectively measure CREATING DEMAND
 
Companies try to innovate on new revenue-generating programs like a podcast, posting content on LinkedIn, evangelism/advocacy or engaging inside communities with their prospective customers, but all of these programs look like they’re failing because companies don’t measure them effectively. 
 
They bring all their demand capture mindset, metrics and tactics into demand creation programs, which is why they never work. 
 
The only way to solve this is to enhance your attribution system to also effectively measure demand creation. Smart companies are recognizing this *measurement gap* and building the appropriate infrastructure to measure demand creation to put their Marketing team in a position to succeed and deliver for the business. 
 
---
 
You get what you measure. Your entire revenue strategy gets built around how you measure success. 
 
Change your mindset -> change your metrics -> get better results.

#marketing #b2b #demand #sales

p.s. this video is the most clearly I've ever explained this measurement gap and how B2B companies could easily solve it. More context in the comments.

A simple copywriting tip for you to write better copies-

It is by using the customer's complaints as your selling point 

But how?

💡 Go to the reviews section of your client's product.
💡Find the low-starred ones. 
💡Pick the keyword of the common issue customers are complaining about 
💡& make it your SELLING POINT!!!

I don't know how many of you uses this tip. But it has been doing wonders for me! 🤯 

#copywritingtips

If you ask a marketer their background story. It will always start off as “I was a content writer, then I moved towards copywriting and now I’m this” 


And here is how content writing helps you develop the skills of a marketer. 

Storytelling: As they say, sometimes people buy the experience and not the product. Every great marketer is a great storyteller!

SEO: It's not enough to write great content; if no one can find it through keywords. Most content writers are good at SEO.

Social Media Marketing: Most content writers know how to create content on social media which is an important thing in times like this. 

Copywriting: Marketing is pointless if you are not being able to sell with your words. 

Proofreading: It is a skill every professional should have. Because even the most talented people need to edit and proofread their work. 

What skills do you think you gained from content writing? 😉

[POST] 2023-02-23 | activity 7034520853543325696 | likes 49 | comments 29 | shares 0
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7034520853543325696

Networking with the right people helped me attend a function that I almost missed. 😱 


Recently I had planned to attend an important function in Ganganagar, Rajasthan but things didn't go as expected. 

My flight from Bangalore to Delhi got delayed, causing me to miss my bus to Ganganagar. 

With no other option, I had to spend the night at the airport, feeling worried about whether I would make it to the function on time.

I searched on popular travel websites but all the available buses were at night, which wouldn't have helped me. 

Feeling worried, I decided to ask for help online. 

I posted an Instagram story at 2 am night and thankfully, some people replied and informed me about the government buses that were available in the morning at 4 am.

One of my long-lost friend also saw my story and came to pick me up from the airport. 

We both rushed to the bus station, had a cup of tea and booked my ticket. 

It was a hectic journey but it taught me how important networking could be.

I finally reached the venue by 5 pm and was able to attend the function, feeling relieved and grateful.

Here is a picture of Nahargarh Fort that I clicked on my trip to Rajasthan for you all to enjoy! ❤️ 

#rajasthan #networking #nahargarhfort

You must be seeing all these people continuously posting on LinkedIn. Ever wondered why? 

Because people are actually getting leads through LinkedIn. Yeah, that’s right!

Let’s take two cases: A & B

B - 

✅An active user of social media 
✅Regularly posted informative content for their audience. 
✅From interesting case studies to relatable memes, 
✅Engages with its followers and establishes a strong online presence.

On the other hand, A didn't believe in the power of social media and thought that simply having a great portfolio was enough to attract potential clients.

Now imagine you're a business owner looking to hire someone for a project. 

Who would you prefer to work with - the person who actively showcases their work on social media or the person who solely relies on their portfolio?

Although A may have great experience and skills, how will potential clients know about it if there's no way for them to discover it? 

This is where personal branding comes in. 

Where other types of marketing might fail, personal branding won't. 

By building a strong online presence:

👉You can get better clients for your business.
👉Attract better opportunities as a college student.
👉Build strong connections 

Interested in building a strong community here on LinkedIn that will not only create your brand presence but will also give you leads? Then let me help you. DM me! And let’s get started 🚀

Here are some examples:


Don’t use: ”Sign up today” 
Use: “Sign up for free”.

Don’t use: ”Follow this guide” 
Use: “Here’s a step-by-step process for you”.

Don’t use: ”Buy our course ” 
Use: “Improve your 𝘴𝘬𝘪𝘭𝘭 𝘯𝘢𝘮𝘦 with our course.”.

Show people the value you add with your product/service.

This ad by Ad World is a hook in itself. 


I had saved this ad for a very long time.


But never got a chance to speak on it.


Today I'm writing about what I liked and disliked about the ad.


What I liked about the ad?


1. The music!!! it is so innovative and quirky I can listen for hours.


2. The way everything is simplified into smaller parts makes it easier to understand. 


3. The first part was quite intriguing and it is mentioned in a very storytelling way targeting the pain points of the audience.


4. The lead generation part. Just wow!! Simple and effective.


5. I loved how they ended it with some humor "pants are optional". 😂 


What I disliked about the ad?


1. If you are way too hooked on the music and colors then you may miss some parts of the ad because of how fast it is. 😜 


2. The text color during the TikTok section did not contrast well with the background color, making it difficult to read.


3. The ad may be too long for viewers who are not interested in ads, so it could have been shorter.

A successful B2B funnel has three steps:

TOFU: Top of the funnel- Awareness + Attention

- Content Marketing
- Social Media
- Paid Awareness
- Brand Building

MOFU: Middle of the funnel- Interest + Consideration

- Industry Reports
- Case Study
- Outbound Emails
- Product Showcase
- Webinars

BOFU: Bottom of the funnel- Intent + Action

- Free trials
- Consultations
- Testimonials
- Quotes and estimates
- Coupons and discounts
- Training and tutorials

A B2B marketing funnel doesn’t finish once you’ve made the sale.

So, what to do post funnel?

The next step is to:

- Maximize your value
- Solving prospect issues
- Showcasing packages

[POST] 2023-04-04 | activity 7048955931564404737 | likes 181 | comments 84 | shares 0
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7048955931564404737

If your likes/comments are not matching the no of impressions means your content is bad. 


I have heard about this multiple times

But is this true? 

If this is happening to you it's not because of the content.

But because of Linkedin's algorithm.

Let's try to break it down in a simple format

Linkedin's algorithm changes every few months. 

And for this reason, you will have to keep changing your content format.

For example,

If carousels work great for you and suddenly you noticed it's not getting enough reach.

It means you need to change the format. 

Try shifting towards text posts or photo posts.

Keep changing your content according to the algo.

Linkedin needs people to make all types of posts. 

So when it notices you are posting a similar content type it decreases your reach. 

It's very important to keep experimenting with the algorithm. 

What are your thoughts on this?

How to not be generic while sending out a connection request 👇 


While sending a connection request it's really important to personalize it.

Why?

Because no matter how many followers the creator/business owner has. It grabs their attention!

I received this note with the word "generic" on it and it instantly grabbed my attention.

Here are some other templates with "hook" that you can use while sending a connection request:

For CEOs/Founders:

"Hey name, I have a quick question regarding your business. Can we connect?"

"Hey name, I really liked your recent ___ and wanted to ask this question ___. Let's connect?"

For Creators:

"Hey name, I liked your comment on ___ post and thought of asking you ___"
"Hey name, I noticed you have grown from ___ to ___ in such a short span. What's your secret?"

These kinds of requests will always get accepted rather than generic ones. 

Because it generates curiosity in their mind.

How do you decide which one is for your business? 


The USP approach may be more effective.

-> If your business has:

1. A lot of competitors
2. Product/service has a standout feature or benefits
3. Less budget for marketing 

The ISP approach may be more effective. 

-> If your business has:

1. Different targets 
2. Diverse range of customers 
3. Customers with different needs and preferences

I closed 2 projects using this method..


If you are getting ghosted after sending a proposal, this is for you..

I was recently having a conversation with Lee Salz..

And he showed me how to close deals 3x faster..

The trick is simple:

When you are on a sales call with a potential client, and they ask you for a proposal.

Here’s what you should do:

-> Never ever email the proposal to your prospect. 
-> Instead, ask them when you can set up a call.
->Tell them your clients have always liked when you walk them through the proposal.
-> Get them on the call and walk them through your proposal.

Here’s why:

-> You get to see their reaction
-> You will know if they are interested
-> They can clear their doubts right there
-> You can ask them their thoughts and the next steps
-> You can ask them if they like the proposal and close the deal right there. 

See, how simple it is? 

Lee Salz is the author of three books and helps people close their deals 3x faster. 

Make sure to follow him on LinkedIn and on Youtube for daily sales tips.

And in case you want to check out his books.

Don’t forget to check my comment section.

I have had over 300+ sales call till now…

And one of the most common questions are:

-> Can you give us some discount
-> X is offering for Y amount 
-> It’s not in our budget 

I used to always lower my price.

Or offer them a 30% discount.

But not anymore!

Here’s what changed:

-> I changed my offer 
-> I collected client testimonials 
-> I made some good case studies 

And now out of maybe 50 calls I still get around 10 such questions.

And here’s what I say to them: You can steal it. 😜

——————

Client: We believe you are too expensive.
 
Me: Oh! Interesting. Can I ask you “too expensive” compared to what?

Client: Compared to X guy.

Me: Well, considering the value and results that we provide this price is nothing. 

But if you think saving a few 100s is more important than the result. 

Then we would ask you to move forward with the X guy and if you are not satisfied with their results you can come to us. 

Does that sound fare?

——————

And that’s it.

Always leave it on the client to decide!

And never every lower your prices.

Have experienced anything similar? Let me know in the comments section! 

Liked this post?

Make sure to follow me! ✨

#sales #salescalls #marketing

-> Who are already looking for your service 
-> People who are in your target location 
-> Less than 100 employee size
-> People in your industry 

Here’s what you need to do: 👇🏻

-> Select the remote option 
-> Tap on the extensions

-> Add the downloaded csv 
-> Then go to all filter
-> Add all the keywords you need
-> click on download

🟢 OutreachGeniusAI

That you will be using to acquire clients on social media like:

🔵 Twitter

🔵 Facebook
🔵 Instagram 

Step2: Make Your No-Brainer Offer

1️⃣ No risk investment
2️⃣ Revenue/commission model
3️⃣Refund model

Do not use this offer if you are not sure if you can get the results.

Always:

✅ Under guarantee
✅ Over results

Example:

1. If you can get them results in 30 days use 90 days as your guarantee.
2. Hey name, I can give you results in days and I don't. I will refund you the whole amount.
3. Hey name, I can help you get results in 90 days. And if we don't we work for free till we give you the results.

Step 3: Launch Mass Outreach Campaign:

-Install DrippiAI 
-Scrape your competitor’s followers

You just need to close 2-3 of them!

-Make shortcuts to your texts

-Start posting lead magnets 
-Set a target of 50 people/day

“We helped our client with 300K sales recently and we don’t wanna gate-keep. Comment if you need the strategy”

-Keep a goal for 50-70 people

-Buy 3 AI avatars from getlia 
-Target with sales nav

-Use Salee to write personalized texts 

25x3= 75/daily
75x5= 375/ weekly 

So, if you get 100 replies and you book 50 of them and close 5 of them that is total.

You need 2 people for this

I booked 50 sales calls last month through cold outreach. Here’s how you can too 👇🏻

Use the torjan horse formula ✅

Here’s how 👇🏻

Structure:

1. Ask no-brainer question
2. Be transparent (it’s a pitch, I want you on a call)
3. Request permission
4. Thumbs up as CTA

Script your Trojan Horse Permission Message below:

- VERSION 1: **Hey {name} ill be honest with you, i have something to pitch you but i dont want to do it without your permission. It can easily help you fill up your calendar with coaching clients in the next month without your doing anything, send me a thumbs up if you’re interested in learning about it**

**Trojan Horse FUP Sequence**

Key points:

- Should be simple & short message
- Goal is to get them to watch the video
- When sending, try replying to your initial trojan horse message (if the platform allows)

Here’s some examples of what to use:

1. Hey firstname, is the above cool with you?
2. Hey firstname, is that permission granted or not?
3. Hey firstname, did you watch that vid I sent?

The goal here is to ask their permission. Because let’s be honest people don’t like cold pitches! 

And always make sure you are reaching out to the people who have a need! 

——

If you are are B2B business owner looking to get 3-5 clients/mo then reach out to me. And let me help you!


#b2bmarketing #torjanhorseframework #leads

You can become a millionaire if you develop a second brain!

All millionaires have a second brain. And here’s how you can too ➡️

1️⃣. Use the 80% Rule:

This Japanese principle is simple: eat until you’re 80% full.

Eating too quickly or too much can cause:

• Brain fog
• Sleepiness
• Poor nutrient absorption

Eat until you’re satiated, not until you’re stuffed.

2️⃣. Get More Polyphenols

Polyphenols are rich in antioxidants, reducing inflammation.

5 great ways to add polyphenols to your diet:

• Cacao
• Green tea
• Flaxseeds
• Blueberries
• Olives (and olive oil)

3️⃣. Categorise the areas where you want to track your goals. It's usually:

➡️ health
➡️ finance
➡️ career

Under each Area..

Define specific goals. 

Under health , you could have a SMART goal like By December 2022, I will lose X kg or improve hba1c to X. 

Under career you could have "I will have completed Project at a level of 8/10" etc. Each area can have multiple goals.

Under each Goal, you can either have

i) Actionable projects or habit routines. These can each be further broken down into specific actionable tasks and even subtasks.

iii) Information/Resource - All the articles, information that can help you achieve this goal does into this folder.

4️⃣ Follow these 5 steps always: 

✅ Capture :Keep a common folder when you immediately capture all information, thoughts etc

✅ Clarify: Convert whatever needs to be done into actionable steps

✅ Organize: Put them into the Area-Goal-Project- Tasks system

✅ Review: Periodically review this system and update it

✅ Engage: Everyday works on the tasks to make progress on the areas.

5️⃣. Choose a note-taking app.

Make folders in P.A.R.A format

Make notes

Progressive summarise those notes if needed for project.

That's all! 


#secondbrain #business #money

[POST] 2024-01-16 | activity 7153045542858600448 | likes 68 | comments 16 | shares 0
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7153045542858600448

Everyone will tell you how to build a SaaS product. But no one will tell you what happens afterward. 

Here are the top 7 pieces of advice I got working with SaaS founders:


1. Things can and will change and evolve over time, but legal, taxes and database schemas are things you want to get right at the start.

2. Be incredibly skeptical and judgemental of anyone you choose to partner up with, just because they are your friend, or seem ‘good’ at some things, does not necessarily mean they are ‘founder’ material. Starting a business with partners is like getting married.

3. Hire slow, fire fast. (Similar to the above)

Role play often with this thought: ‘If I had a gun to my head and absolutely must make $1000 additional profit this week, how would I do it?’ Write down every option available. Hot tip, if you already have revenue, sometimes it can be much easier to cut costs than to find new customers.

4. Every day Choose to work on the hard thing, that thing that’s really important but you keep putting it off because it’s hard… (you know what it is!) All the bloody time you have founders feeling good talking about how they’ve achieved so much, bought a domain, created a logo, built a landing page… all of those things are EASY and most of the time a distraction! 

5. Write the documentation, record the tutorials, make that phone call you’ve been putting off, fix that complicated date/timezone bug customers have been hassling you about… Go and get customers, bang on doors, make the ads, go and talk to every single person you know, and try and find a customer anywhere you can, whatever that hard thing is you’ve been putting off is what you should be working on right now.

6. Start selling. NOW!! If you can log in to your product and barely use it, that’s an MVP! Stop trying to perfect and fine-tune it, and get out there ASAP and see if customers will use it, ask questions, find out why they first clicked, ask them why they wouldn’t pay for it, and get their feedback. Just get out there. Save the finesse and refining as a reward for your efforts. 

(You get to perfect and finesse your product once you get a few customers)


7. Your annoying customers are the valuable ones at the start. The ones that care enough about your product to give you feedback, tell you how it could be better, report bugs, etc. milk them, milk them for every piece of feedback they have to give.

Do you have anything else to add here? Let me know down in the comments below ⬇️

90% of the B2B SaaS platforms will fail in 2024! 

If you don’t have a clear GTM strategy! 

Let’s break it down in 3 pillars:

Ask yourself this ⬇️

1. Market Strategy:

➡️ What is the market you are going for? 
➡️ Who is your (niche) ideal customer persona? 
➡️ How big is the total addressable market TAM? 
➡️ What is the big problem you're trying to solve?

2. Product Strategy 

➡️ What's the solution for the core problem you're trying to solve? 
➡️ How to make sure your customers get immediate value with your product - getting them to AHA moment fast

3. Growth Strategy 

➡️ How do you grow? 
➡️ Are you marketing-driven vs. sales-driven vs. product-led driven? 
➡️ What growth channels work for you? 
What is your positioning (market segmentation), your value proposition (sales messaging), and your pricing strategy (cheapest vs. most expensive?)

And write everything down! See, where you are lacking and what needs to be improved! 

Once you are done. Make your strategy! 

Here are 7 steps for creating your go-to-market strategy:

- Launching is a stage, NOT a one-time event

- Build an (engaged) audience from the beginning

- Know your ideal customer personas' BIG problem

- Why now? What are the macro trends supporting your business?

- Value proposition instead of direct selling yourself in the market

- Growth activities (inbound, outbound, content, ads)

- Leverage product-led growth

———

I recently spoke to a SaaS founder making $1M in ARR 

And this is what he mentioned 👇 


1. Build a waiting list on Discord instead of email marketing.

2. Partner with skilled people for growth instead of building a team.

3. Launch a waiting list even before you release the MVP.

4. Use VPN and price parity on the pricing page for fair pricing worldwide.

5. Offer a 7-day trial for B2B SaaS instead of 14 days.

6. Create hype and do demand gen before adding any new features. 

7. Good UI/UX is important no matter how good is your product.

8. Podcasts are as important as blogs now for SaaS especially in 2024

The most difficult part of being an agency owner is…

Hiring! 

90% of you may say it’s sales.

But for me it’s hiring! 

Especially when you are a content creator.

As, most people come for the experience! 

We onboarded a $5000 client last month. 

Everything was going well! 

But then one of our managers quit. 

In between the project! 

They mentioned they wanted start their own agency now.

After that it became difficult for us to handle. 

We assigned the project to another team member (who was not the right fit). 

That led to bad results. 

The client was disappointed. 

And eventually they left us! 

We offered them a 50% refund and a 50% off if they wanted to continue in the future for all the loss caused!

Big projects = Big responsibilities 

We have a 90% success rate! 

But still things like these happen. 

And here’s what I learned from here:

➡️ No matter what anyone says when it comes to hiring do a proper background check. 

(we hired someone who was talented but had no proper degree)

➡️ Have a lawyer (just incase things like these happen)

➡️ Just paying them well isn’t enough. Make sure to ask their intention of joining the company. 

➡️ Get them on a proper probation period before having them sign the contract with client.

➡️ Make sure they have 3+ yrs of work experience (70% 1-2 years work experience people are unprofessional)

And the most important thing…

➡️ Have atleast 3 team members of same level so that atleast one can manage if another cannot. 


I hate the rat race thing. And for this reason we give freedom and a wfh opportunity. 

But it’s important to keep a track on everything. 

We are using Monitask to keep a track on our team!

My core skills are: 

-> lead gen
-> sales 

But hiring is something that makes me go bananas! 

Founders in my network pls drop some good advice when it comes to hiring.

PS: We tried hiring a HR agency as well (that went to hell).

As we were spending 70% of our time doing all the work.
——

Thoughts on this?

#hiring #team #entrepreneurship

Is hiring an agency better or having an in-house team?

Let's talk!

It's always interesting to hear what people have to say on this.

Here’s my take: 👇

Benefits of hiring an agency:

- Low investment high returns
 -Save time
- Faster Turnaround

Benefits of having an in-house team:

- Immediate availability 
- Better understanding of your business
- Better control and adaptability 

Which is the best option for your business?

1️⃣. If your MRR is <$20K:

Hiring an agency will work the best. 

Here you won’t have to invest a lot of time in team training.
All you have to care is about the results 

But make sure to look at the agency’s portfolio before signing them.

2️⃣. If your MRR is $20K+:

Build your own in-house team!

You can hire an agency as well here to train your team and build SOPs as a one-time thing!

[POST] 2024-03-07 | activity 7171495822629498880 | likes 120 | comments 26 | shares 2
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7171495822629498880

Get 500 leads in 5 mins for absolutely free ⬇ 

1. Go to google:

➡ Settings 
➡ Search setting 
➡ Increase result per page to 100

2. Type this: 

➡ Type in site: 

LinkedIn. com "founder" "@ gmail. com 

➡ You can do this with platforms like:

>Twitter
>Instagram
>Youtube

3. To get all their emails at once:

➡ Copy all the data from Google
➡ Go to Emailcheck.net
➡ Click on menu
➡ Click on the email extractor
➡ Paste the data
➡ Click on extract
➡ Now emails get separated
➡ Copy and paste them into an Excel sheet

4. To verify the emails for free:

➡ Go to Excel sheet
➡ Click on "Insert"
➡ Click on "smart chips"
➡ Click on "convert to people chip"

The ones that turn into a smart chip are verified.

And voila you have verified the email address.

Make sure to use this strategy with very personalized emails. 

----

Follow for more such content on lead-gen!

#leadgeneration #leadership #leads

LinkedIn's connection notes suck! 

Especially in 2024

Do not send a connection request with an "attached note" 

Unless it's necessary...

People hate it when you pitch in the first msg itself. 

Do this instead:

1. Remove what you do from your LinkedIn's headline 

2. Only add founder/building @ (your company name) 

3. Use this template only when it's needed "Hey name, I just saw your post on ___ (one-liner personalization note). I agree with X and wanted to ask (ask them a question regarding their post).

4. Always warm them up you can do this by engaging with their content or sending them free resources. 

5. After approx 1 week of them accepting your request you can soft-pitch them. 

Remember sales is old-school.

This message landed in spam!

It's 2024, what's the point of sending such long messages?

Here are the red flags I spotted:

🚩 Way too long
🚩 No right ICP (digital marketing agency but provides project management)
🚩 Intro in the first para
🚩 Asking for a call in the first msg
🚩 No mention of why I would need their service.

These 5 agency hacks will 3x your revenue in 90 days. (GURANTEED) 

(The last one is the best tip)

1. Hire good people:

It’s always okay to charge more from your clients 

If you can deliver the results!

Remember:

Your team quality gives you client retention. 

And client retention speaks about your service delivery!

2. Sell systems instead of service:

This is the easiest way to scale.

If you already know the work! 

The best part? 

You don’t need a huge team or a lot of clients. 

One project deal can be: $7K-$30K 

This offer is best for lead-gen agencies. 

3. Create a downsell offer: 

Not all your leads are qualified. 

Especially if they are inbound! 

So instead of reffering them to someone else or letting them go…

Do this 👇🏻

Make a one time build and release offer for them! 

That could be around $49-$299/mo 

Best way? 

Skool/discord communities! 

4. Have a refund policy: 

Now this may sound risky! 

But here’s what you can do to make it less risky: 

Charge a small amount called set-up fee which will be:

✅ your team
✅ softwares 

(Best if your team is in Philippines or India) 

Keep this as non-refundable! 

Charge good amount. 

Like if you don’t get this result get X refund. 

For example: 

- Charge $1000 or something as set-up fee and $5000 as your service charge! 

Here even if you need to refund them you are still not losing much because your team will get paid anyway! 

Make sure to be perfect in your skill if you’re going to offer this! 

5. Get a referral policy in your contract:

Hands down best way to scale 🙌🏻

Example: 

Make an offer of $10,000 give $5000 off if they refer you to 5 people. 

Offer the service for free if they refer you to 10 people. 

But..

Make sure to do this from premium clients or influencers. 

Don’t offer this to brokies they will have other brokies in their network! 

——

And yeah that’s all. 

Do this for 30 days atleast and see how you scale! 

Follow Suprava for more such tips! 👈🏻

I sent 100 cold DMs every day for 17 months.

1. Not having a connection note gives you better acceptance rate 
2. Direct pitching never works in the first msg 
3. Cold DMs only work when the prospect has a buying intent

6. Your prospects can always tell you are about to sell to them

[RESHARE] 2024-06-17 | activity 7208480024767737858 | likes 81 | comments 12 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7208480024767737858
(Reshared from: Priyanka S)

Write cold emails like the top 1%

just make it about them, not you.


Picture this: a stranger on the street approaches you and starts talking only about oneself.

Why should you care?

The same applies to cold emails.

Rather than dwelling on some details, make your message relevant to your prospects.

Begin by demonstrating how your email pertains to their situation - what prompted you to reach out?

Paint a vivid picture of the problem they might be facing.

Then, present your solution as the answer to their dilemma.

Ask if the issue you've described resonates with them.


Thoughts? 🤓

70/100 people land on spam while cold-emailing 

Here's why: 👇 

❌ No DNS set-up. 
❌ Using links in the first email copy. 
❌ 100+ words in the first email copy. 
❌ Not using Apollo.io's keyword feature. 
❌ Asking for a meeting in the first email. 
❌ Using spam words like "save x$ in 60 days." 
❌ Having a link attached to their email signature. 
❌ Giving a long para on why their company is best.
❌ Starting the email copy with "hope this email finds you well." 

Here's how you can identify if you are landing on spam 👇 

Sign up for MailReach and check your score 

If the score is 10/10:

✅ Good news you are not landing on spam!

But if your score looks something like 9/10,7/10 or less:

📧🚫 Then you are landing on spam

Few things you can do to improve your deliverability rate:

- Do proper infra set-up
- Use Google Workspace 
- Use Trigify.io to identify sales triggers
- Use MxToolBox to check DNC records 
- Do double verification using MillionVerifier

Need my help setting up your cold email infrastructure?

Shoot me a DM!

Find decision-makers of funded companies ⬇ 

Step 1:

Make a list of funded companies using Crunchbase

Step 2:

Install Instant data scraper

Step 3:

Scrape the Data: Use Instant Data Scraper to crawl and download data as a CSV file.

Step 4:

Add Your Data to Clay: Upload the CSV to Clay, organize, and clean up the data.

Step 5:

Find the Company Domain: Enrich your data with company domains using Clearbit integration in Clay.

Step 6:

Find the Decision Makers: Use the company domains to identify decision makers by job titles in Clay.

Step 7:

Get the Email Addresses of Decision Makers using Clay agent

Use Clay's 'Recipe' to combine integrations and get all relevant info in one step.

The SMB recipe provides the decision maker’s:

- name
- job title
- active work email.

The waterfall method is ideal for specific data searches like names or email addresses.

- Add enrichment to your table and select the 'Waterfall' tab.
- Choose 'Work Email' to retrieve email addresses.

Use:

- Clay Enrichments
- Datagma
- Hunter.io

as an email provider.

Set the contact's name and domain as inputs.

Validate emails using DeBounce.

[POST] 2024-07-29 | activity 7223676961011728385 | likes 139 | comments 30 | shares 8
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7223676961011728385

Steal competitor’s customers 

And get 30-40 leads per week!

(Specifically for SaaS founders)

This workflow have been used by companies like Clay.

Copy this workflow into your GTM system

1. Re-target Competitor’s Customers:

This is one of the best ways to find your target audience, especially if you’re new to the market.

Steps:

1. Use BuiltWith to find the software your ideal customers (ICP) are using.
2. Enrich this list with Persana AI

With this enriched list, you can start your outreach or ad campaigns.

2. Influencer Marketing Without Spending Big Bucks:

The right influencer can significantly impact your business. 
According to HubSpot, influencer marketing is a fast track for SaaS founders, but it can be tough if you’re bootstrapped.

What to Do Instead:

1. Identify micro-influencers with a relevant audience using Taplio
2. List their competitors.

Show these influencers how their competitors are benefiting from your product and offer them a free 3-month plan in exchange for creating posts for you.

Create FOMO by showcasing real-time results, like "I got 30 leads using X product."

3. Content Strategy That Generates Leads:

Don’t just talk about your product. 

Engage your audience with challenges that are relevant to your ICP.

Example:

Start a challenge in a community (like Skool) that fits your product. 

For example, if your product helps SMBs get leads, create a "100 sales calls in 9 weeks" challenge using your product.

Invite people to join, make it competitive, and limit the number of participants you can personally help.

Weekly, ask participants to post their progress and experiences with your product on LinkedIn.

This will help with faster demand gen without spending a lot.

(For more detailed content plans, comment or DM me!)

4. Outreach Campaigns:

Pick a platform where your ICP hangs out. 

For example, LinkedIn.

Steps:

1. Use Taplio to find lead magnets.

One of my clients lost $50K 

I was recently on a client call 

They hired a well-known sales guru to train their sales reps

Expecting a major boost in performance. 

Unfortunately, it turned out to be a complete disaster. 

The training was too basic and didn't address the real issues.

After the training, there was a wave of firings because the sales team wasn’t hitting their quotas. 

But later on, they realized the problem wasn't with the salespeople at all. 

The real issues were:

- A poor-quality leads list
- Weak email infrastructure

They thought the outreach script and sales pitch not up to the mark

But the real issue was their email setup:

- They had 10 email accounts all under one domain.
- They were sending over 50 emails daily from a single account.
- Worst of all, they were reaching out to people who weren’t even the decision-makers.

This poor setup led to:

- Massive firings
- $30K wasted
- 30% drop in quota attainment.

Before pointing fingers at your sales reps

Make sure they have a solid lead generation system that’s up to date for 2024.

Most sales reps aren’t experts in the technical aspects of building a high-quality list, so investing in the right infrastructure is key.

[POST] 2024-09-25 | activity 7244681716211081216 | likes 66 | comments 26 | shares 1
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7244681716211081216

How to track open rate without landing on spam? 

Have you ever noticed that emails with links often end up in spam? 

The image I’ve attached shows what happens when you include any links in your email.

This is because Google uses an invisible pixel to track opens. 

When your prospect opens the email, the pixel reports it as “opened.”

However, recent updates have made these pixels more likely to trigger spam filters.

That makes your emails to land in your prospect’s spam folder. 

So, what can you do?

Option 1: Stop using open tracking – but this leaves you without data.

Option 2: Use ReachInbox’s new feature! 

They’ve developed a solution that allows you to track opens without landing in spam, even when using tracking pixels.

——

If you found this helpful, share it with someone who could benefit!

#opentracking #coldemail #reachinbox

[POST] 2024-09-27 | activity 7245393267037491200 | likes 112 | comments 28 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7245393267037491200

Most common issues in lead generation 👇🏻

1. Only relying on referrals:

While referrals have the highest closing rate. 

You cannot scale your business with just referrals.

(Unless you are in super niched sectors)

2. Cold emails from main domain:

One of the huge mistake is using your primary domain to send cold emails. 

I see this practice among age group 30-50.

You risk your main domain getting blacklisted. 

3. Not building a brand:

Does not matter if you are building a company or a personal brand. 

Make sure to provide value.

[RESHARE] 2024-10-31 | activity 7257653710451818496 | likes 32 | comments 8 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7257653710451818496
(Reshared from: Evgeny Kotelevskiy)

What’s the optimal number of mailboxes per domain for cold emails?

I’ve seen various recommendations, with the average number being around three.

The idea is simple: using fewer mailboxes helps decrease spam complaints, which, in turn, improves your domain’s reputation.

I used to create three mailboxes, then switched to two. Recently, I joined a community led by Suprava Sabat (which I highly recommend), and they suggested creating just one mailbox per domain.

I also recently came across some interesting stats from one of my projects. I had five mailboxes in Lemwarm: two mailboxes on one domain, two on another, and a single mailbox on a third domain.

After three months, one domain (with two mailboxes) was blacklisted. The second domain, also with two mailboxes, had moderate results. Meanwhile, the third domain - with just one mailbox - performed the best, maintaining an 87 Lemwarm score and the lowest spam rate.

All three domains had the same setup:

- Microsoft 360
- Open rate tracking disabled
- A maximum of 30 cold emails per day, plus 25 warm-up emails

The takeaway: minimize the number of mailboxes per domain. I’ll be testing this further with other accounts, but for now, it seems that having just one email per domain is the best approach.

#dedicadoagency

How you can make cold-email work 👇🏻

A lot of people over complicate cold-emails

They think it’s highly technical

With all the domains and google restrictions.

But it’s really not!

I got my first high paying client ($2500) through cold emailing

When I was a freelancer I did not had a domain

I used to cold email prospects from a gmail account.

And that’s how I used to get clients.

I used to send 30 highly personalised cold emails daily

And out of those I used to get at least 5 positive replies

My emails used to be long but it had a good offer.

I used to offer first 5 leads for absolutely free

And I was targeting sales coaches back then!

My offer had:

- Linkedin marketing
- Lead generation
- Webinar funnel

But times change what used to work back then

May not work now.

At AcquisitionX we send 300K+ emails every month

And here is what’s working for us:

1. Having variations: 

We have mail accounts from google, microsoft and third party tools like Maildoso.

So in case, if one is not working we always have a backup!

Similarly we do not rely on only one software for scraping leads. 

We use 3-4 softwares and keep adding new ones.

We have a person who manually scrapes a few things like websites, communities and a few other portals. 

Then we enrich and send emails.

2. Using personalisation with relevancy:

Most people think that they can just mention a random life event they posted about and think that their prospects will respond.

Example:

Hey x, saw you guys went to a boat party with your team hope you had fun now here’s what im selling…..

This may not work! ❌

Here’s what we do:

✅ Personalisation only when the subject is relevant

Example: hey x, you commented on my post that x is an issue for you guys! What if I can help you solve x in y weeks? I’m only saying this because we have helped clients like a,b,c solve the same problem!

I would love to send you more information on this you can send it to your team as well. Let me know!

Now if you see the second example has relevancy on to why we are reaching out.

3. Having private webinars:

Instead of asking them for a call.

We reached out to them saying that we are hosting a private webinar with only 5 participants.

This makes them feel special plus we don't have to jump on 5 separate calls in one hour. 

We explain the funnel to those 5 participants with a crazy upsell and downsell offer then we proceed if they are interested.

And that’s what!

We keep changing our funnel every month.

Why your SDR isn’t booking calls 👇

Most B2B companies blame their SDRs for poor closing rates.

But the problem?

It’s often not the SDR—it’s the leads or the email setup.

Here’s the usual thought process:

	•	If my SDR contacts 100 people, they should book at least 5 calls.
	•	Training them on “relationship-based selling” will fix it.
	•	Let’s buy expensive software to solve this.
	•	My AE isn’t converting; they must be bad at their job.

But no one checks the lead quality or email infrastructure.

Here are some myths I see daily:

1️⃣ Contacting 100 leads doesn’t guarantee calls—quality > quantity.

2️⃣ Training SDRs is fine, but they can’t close if the leads are outdated.

3️⃣ Relying solely on databases like Apollo or ZoomInfo? So is everyone else. What sets you apart?

4️⃣ Cold email in 2024 is tougher than ever—one setup isn’t enough. You need at least 3 setup variations.

The real issues:

	•	Low-quality lead lists
	•	Weak email infrastructure

I’ve seen teams with:

	•	10 email accounts under one domain
	•	50+ emails sent daily per account
	•	Reaching out to people who weren’t even decision-makers

Before blaming your sales reps, ask yourself:

❓ Is your leads list up to date for 2024?
❓ Are you using tools like Maildoso to optimize your outreach infrastructure?

Most SDRs aren’t trained to build high-quality lists or manage complex setups. That’s where the real fix begins.

Here are some good alternatives for apollo/zoominfo:

- Prospeo.io 

As per cold email infrastructure have 3 variations minimum:

1. Google 
2. Microsoft 
3. Third party: In my experience, this has been working best for us you can check out Maildoso

Warm emails will kill cold email in 2025

Let’s be honest we are getting daily updates.

On how google is trying to kill cold email.

And the rules will only get more strict from here.

And I agree with the rules as someone who runs a lead gen agency!

The reason is simple.

So many people are now blatantly spamming in the name of outbound.

Scrape from websites like apollo—> send emails.

I'm guilty as well for this!

For which, warm emails will work more than cold email in 2025.

Here’s how you can implement this to your system:

🟪 Attract traffic to your website and retarget:

Just having a website is useless.

A better way is having lead magnets in your website.

And then advertising those lead magnets in communities and forums for example.

For example: We make a lead magnet on how to book x amount of sales calls and then created posts on subreddits, discord channels and skool communities.

It got us a good amount of traffic which we later reached out to them using RB2B 

🟪 Target followers:

Now these followers could be your own if you don’t have any use your competitor’s followers.

We use Phantom buster and a fake linkedin account to scrape

But you can check out ScrapeLi  just read their post I think the team is doing great work.

If you do this make sure to enrich the profiles!

🟪 Track social signals:

You must have seen posts like “I’m looking for a GTM expert or hiring for a GTM expert”.

You can track posts like these using Trigify.io  

🟪 Linkedin inbound funnel:

If you are in b2b then this funnel can alone bring you solid results.

You can create lead magnets using Distribute  

Automate the msg sending using LeadShark 🦈  

Use Persana AI to enrich the profiles.

🟪 Newsletter subscribers retarget:

Maybe only 1-2% of your subscribers read your newsletter

But if you run a specific campaign for a specific audience you can see great results.

You can use Substack or beehiiv to build the infrastructure.

And make specific campaigns for this!

And that’s how you can build your warm acquisition funnel for 2025.

Upload your documents, YouTube links, and files.

After sending 100,000+ cold emails last year, 

Here’s what I realised:

Your offer and copy don’t matter 

If your emails never reach the inbox.

The most important part of cold email success is deliverability.

Why?

Even the best emails won’t work if they land in spam.

A great offer is pointless if your domain gets blocked.

Here’s what matters first:

1️⃣ Proper secondary domain setup to protect your main domain.
2️⃣ DKIM settings to ensure your emails are authenticated.
3️⃣ Smart IP rotation to avoid getting flagged as spam.
4️⃣ Deliverability health monitoring to catch issues before they hurt your campaigns.

That’s where Maildoso helps—

It keeps emails on track and out of spam.

Everyone is talking about AI sales agents 

But no one is teaching you how to build one.

And even if they do it’s too much tech work.

Recently my team and I are experimenting with AI agents 

And we have tested a few no-code softwares.

Here’s the most easiest way to build an agent that does:

✅ Research leads
✅ Make personalized calls
✅ Qualify prospects over the phone
✅ Update your CRM in real-time
✅ Send hyper-personalized follow-ups on WhatsApp, email, and LinkedIn

Lemme show you the easiest way to build one:

1️⃣ Start with Lead Capture:

Use Typeform or any lead form tool to collect initial information (e.g., name, email, needs).

Trigger the workflow using Make to connect your form with Relevance AI.

2️⃣ Automate Lead Research:

Use the Find LinkedIn module in Relevance AI to gather lead details.

Scrape LinkedIn profiles and websites to build a summary.

Example output:

Name: Brian Chesky
Role: Co-Founder, Airbnb
Need: Sales automation for personalized email outreach

3️⃣ Add Personalized Voice Calls

Build a voice-calling feature using Relevance AI’s voice tool or integrate with Vapy.

Create dynamic, personalized scripts based on lead research.

Example Script:

“Hi Brian, I’m Robert, Ben’s AI assistant. I saw you’re looking for sales automation. Could you share what specific challenges you’d like to solve?”

Include decision points in the call:

Qualified? Proceed to schedule a meeting.
Not Qualified? Suggest other resources or partners.

4️⃣ Follow Up Across Channels

Automatically send personalized follow-ups through:

WhatsApp: Use Relevance AI’s WhatsApp integration to send messages from your personal or business account.

Email: Automate customized emails (e.g., “Here’s my calendar link for a quick chat!”).

LinkedIn: Send connection invites with tailored messages.

5️⃣ Automate CRM Updates

Integrate with tools like HubSpot or Salesforce. (or your preferred software) 

Log every interaction:

Lead details
Call outcomes
Next steps

Example log:

“Brian Chesky expressed interest in email automation and content creation for LinkedIn. Budget approved. Meeting scheduled.”

[POST] 2025-02-03 | activity 7292133951945543682 | likes 127 | comments 46 | shares 2
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7292133951945543682

I watched someone close a $10K deal in the DMs

Without:

- aggressive pitching
- endless follow-ups
- desperate “just checking in” emails.

I had to know how they did it.

The founder said 

That the client was watching their content for over 3 months. 

And was already 90% sold.

- The client had seen their posts. 
- Read their newsletter .
- Saw their free resource. 

So when the time came to have a conversation, trust was already built.

Here’s everything he told me.

And that we are implementing in 2025: (copy it)

🟩Step 1: Make Them Notice You (Without Begging)

The first rule of high-ticket sales: 

If they don’t know you, 

they won’t buy from you.

So before you even think about pitching, they need to see your name, your content, your insights—everywhere.

LinkedIn  & X: Drop lead magnets that feel too valuable to be free.

Reddit, Inc. - Post in niche subs, but keep it real. 

No promo, just straight-up valuable insights.

YouTube & Instagram: Quick, snappy videos breaking down one problem they’re stuck on.

The Goal? 

You want them thinking, “Damn, I keep seeing this person everywhere.”

🟩Step 2: Keep Them Warm (So They Remember You)

Getting noticed once isn’t enough. 

You need to stay in their brain without being annoying.

Community (Skool, Discord, Slack): 

Create a space where they actually WANT to hang out.

Share behind-the-scenes insights, private case studies, or industry shifts.

Newsletter (Substack) 

Keep dropping fire in their inbox—not corporate nonsense, just practical, tactical stuff.


Why? 

Because when you finally DM them, 

they already know you. 


🟩Step 3: Close Without Being ‘Salesy’

By now, they trust you. 

Here’s how you lock in the deal without sounding like a despo.

Cold Emails & DMs: No pitch-slapping. 

Instead, collaborate before you sell.

“Hey [Name], I’m writing about [Industry Problem] and would love your take. Up for a quick chat?”

Now they’re on a call with you—

without feeling like they’re being sold to.

Webinars: Get them in for free value, then naturally introduce your offer.

LinkedIn DMs: Build convos instead of pushing products.

“Loved your take on [Topic]. Curious—what’s your biggest challenge with [Thing] right now?”

The Secret?

Everything Feeds Into Everything.

This system works because all the pieces build on each other:

1️⃣ Someone finds your LinkedIn post → Signs up for a free resource

2️⃣ They get your emails → Join your community

3️⃣ They watch your webinar → Boom, they’re ready to buy

Do this, and leads will chase YOU.

[POST] 2025-02-04 | activity 7292507603136372736 | likes 104 | comments 32 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7292507603136372736

The $20K cold email mistake ⬇️

A founder I know went all in on cold email.   He did everything right:

✅ Hired a team
✅ Bought lead lists from Upwork 
✅ Invested in top-rated tools
✅ Spent hours crafting the perfect emails

After three months? 

Barely five calls booked.

And they weren’t even great calls

At first, he thought maybe the offer wasn’t strong enough. 

Or that his targeting needed tweaking. 

But when he checked his deliverability…

80% of his emails never even made it to inboxes.

Turns out, the problem wasn’t his messaging or his offer—it was his email setup.

Like a lot of founders, he went with a cheap SMTP provider. 

It seemed like a great deal—quick setup, “premium” deliverability, low cost. 

But in reality:

❌ Emails were getting blocked before anyone could even open them

❌ Spam filters caught them because of overloaded shared servers

❌ Customer support told him, “Your copy is the problem” instead of admitting their servers were flagged

If you’re serious about cold email, don’t waste months wondering why it’s not working.

This is why using tools like Maildoso can help:

✅ Private, clean IPs (no spammy shared servers)

✅ Expert support that actually helps fix deliverability, not blame your copy

✅ Inbox placement tracking so you know exactly where your emails land

——

Cold email works if your prospects actually receive your emails.




#automatedcoldemail #coldemailsetup

Most outbound emails flop—

Here’s why:

Ever read a cold email and thought, 

why are you even reaching out to me?

Yeah, same.

Most outbound emails don’t fail because the offer is bad. 

They fail because they don’t make sense to the person reading them.

Here’s where they go wrong:

🚩 Fake Personalization – “I noticed your recent comment…” No, you didn’t. You scraped it and threw it into your outreach tool.

🚩 Forced Relevance – “It got me thinking about how companies like yours might be evaluating solutions for international expansion.” No real connection. Just a weak transition into a pitch.

🚩 The Premature Pitch – “At [Company], we help businesses like yours do XYZ.” Cool, but I didn’t ask.

🚩 Overly Specific Questions – “Are you currently exploring options for expanding internationally?” The odds of me saying “yes” right now are slim. But if you asked whether hiring internationally is a challenge, I might actually engage.

How to fix it (And get more replies):

✅ Lead with relevance, not randomness
If you’re going to reference something I said, make sure it actually ties into your email.

✅ Start a conversation, not a sales pitch
Instead of jumping straight to “We help companies do XYZ,” try “A lot of teams struggle with X—curious if that’s been an issue for you?”

✅ Make it easy to reply
A simple yes/no question like “Is this something you deal with?” is way easier to answer than “Are you currently looking for a solution?”

——

Good outbound isn’t about hacking your way into someone’s inbox. 

It’s about making it worth replying to.

Cold calling is dead. 

Make your leads call you instead.

Here’s how: ⬇️

Most cold calls fail before they even start. 

Not because your pitch is bad—but because the prospect wasn’t expecting it.

Sales isn’t about interrupting anymore. 

It’s about attracting.

Here’s how top sellers are getting more booked calls without chasing prospects:

Step 1: Make Them Come to You:

Nobody wants to get a random pitch in their inbox. 

Instead, give them a reason to reach out.

Try this post:

"I've been helping [ICP] fix [pain point] without [common struggle]. If you're dealing with this, drop a comment or DM me—I’ll walk you through how to solve it."

Why this works:

- No pressure. It’s framed as a free consultation, not a sales pitch.

- Self-qualification. Only people with real interest will book.

- Inbound positioning. You’re not chasing; they’re coming to you.

Step 2: The Call Shouldn’t Feel Like a Sales Call

Most reps talk too much. 

The best ones listen.

Flip the script:

- Ask the right questions. 
- Let them tell you their pain points.
- Challenge their approach. 
- Show them why what they’re doing isn’t working.
- Give them a small win. 
- If they see value, they’ll want more.

If you make the call about them (not you), they’ll be the ones asking how to work with you.

Step 3: Automate the Entire Process

This works, but it’s even better when you don’t have to do it manually.

- Persana AI pulls contact info from those who engage.
- LeadShark 🦈 auto-DMs them with a follow-up.
- Smartlead sends warm email sequences.

No manual scraping. 
No spam. 

Just high-intent leads booking calls.

Why This Works?

Cold calls interrupt. ❌
This attracts. ✅

Pitching upfront creates resistance. 

Giving value creates demand.

Instead of chasing leads, you become the person they seek out.

By 2027, the best salespeople won’t be the ones making the most calls.

They’ll be the ones getting the most inbound.

If cold calling is still part of your strategy, it might be time to rethink how you're getting prospects on the phone.

How AI SDRs actually work?

AI SDRs do more than just send emails—they:

- identify leads
- analyze intent
- personalize outreach
- qualify prospects
- book meetings 

all at scale. 

Here’s how they work 

And the tools that power them:

1️⃣ Lead Identification & Data Enrichment:

AI finds and enriches leads using:

RB2B → Identifies anonymous website visitors.
Breakcold → Checks CRM to avoid duplicate outreach.
Trigify.io & Leadspicker → Tracks job changes, funding rounds, and hiring trends.

AI scans LinkedIn, job boards, and databases to find buyers

Adds context like company news, and ensures outreach is fresh.

2️⃣ Understanding the Prompt & Generating Outreach:

AI SDRs don’t just send generic messages. 

They follow structured prompts that define:

- Goal (book a call, follow up, gather info).
- Lead details (company, role, activity, pain points).
- Tone & personalization (casual, direct, professional).

AI also retrieves past conversations to maintain context.

3️⃣ Prioritization & Intent Detection:

Not every lead is worth chasing!

AI qualifies and ranks prospects based on engagement.

How It works:

- AI analyzes email opens, LinkedIn engagement, and CRM signals to score intent.
- High-intent leads move to a high-touch sequence with immediate follow-ups.
- Unresponsive leads are dropped or nurtured passively to avoid wasting time.

4️⃣ Handling Conversations & Lead Qualification:

AI SDRs respond over email, chat, or voice and qualify leads using:

Humanlinker & Amplemarket → Video and voice note-based LinkedIn outreach.

BANT framework: to assess Budget, Authority, Need, and Timeline.

If a lead fits, AI books a meeting or routes it to a human SDR.

5️⃣ Automated Follow-Ups & Smart Nurturing:

Follow-ups are adjusted based on engagement:

HeyReach → LinkedIn DMs.
Drippi.ai → Twitter outreach.
Cold DM → Instagram messaging.
Smartlead / Instantly.ai → Cold email

AI changes messaging angle and outreach channel (email → LinkedIn → Twitter) to improve response rates.

6️⃣ CRM Integration & Learning:

Persana AI / Airscale / Clay → Optimize targeting and refine outreach.

Tracks what’s working and adjusts messaging automatically.

7️⃣ Multichannel Outreach:

If email fails, 

AI shifts to another channel where the lead is active.

They find, engage, and qualify leads automatically, 

Allowing human reps to focus on closing deals. 

If you're not using AI for outbound, you're already behind.

How I replaced clay using just google sheets

If you have a list of company names and need their websites for outreach, 

Here’s how to automate the process inside Google Sheets

Step 1: Turn Google Sheets → Web Scraper

Open Google Sheets→ Extensions→ Apps 

ScriptCopy-paste this script:

const base_url_clearbit = "https://lnkd.in/day3S7Xt";  
function clearbit_getDomain(name) {  
  return UrlFetchApp.fetch(base_url_clearbit +encodeURIComponent(name)).getContentText();}

Save & run it.

Step 2: Company → Name Websites

Use this formula in your sheet:

=clearbit_getDomain(A2)

Your sheet will auto-fill with company websites—no manual searching required.

Step 3: Find Decision-Makers

Use LinkedIn Sales Navigator filters to search by company domain and find key employees.

Use email finders like Prospeo.io or Findymail to get verified contacts.

This method gives you:

✔️ Company websites for your outbound efforts
✔️ A scalable, efficient process without high costs
✔️ More control over your data without depending on paid tools

And that’s it!

docker --version

If it shows a version number, you’re good to go! ✅

🔹 Step 3: Get Your Claude AI API Key

	•	Go to Claude Console
	•	Click “Get API Key” and save it (keep it private!)

	•	Claude gives $5 free credits—add more if needed.
	•	Set billing limits to avoid unexpected charges.

🔹 Step 5: Link Your API Key

For Mac/Linux:

For Windows:

$env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="your_api_key"

🔹 Step 6: Run Your AI Agent

docker run -e ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=$ANTHROPIC_API_KEY \
-it ghcr. io/anthropics/anthropic-quickstarts:computer-use-demo-latest

How you can make cold-email work 👇🏻

A lot of people over complicate cold-emails

They think it’s highly technical

With all the domains and google restrictions.

But it’s really not!

I got my first high paying client ($2500) through cold emailing

When I was a freelancer I did not had a domain

I used to cold email prospects from a gmail account.

And that’s how I used to get clients.

I used to send 30 highly personalised cold emails daily

And out of those I used to get at least 5 positive replies

My emails used to be long but it had a good offer.

I used to offer first 5 leads for absolutely free

And I was targeting sales coaches back then!

My offer had:

- Linkedin marketing
- Lead generation
- Webinar funnel

But times change what used to work back then

May not work now.

At AcquisitionX we send 300K+ emails every month

And here is what’s working for us:

1. Having variations: 

We have mail accounts from google, microsoft and third party tools like Maildoso.

So in case, if one is not working we always have a backup!

Similarly we do not rely on only one software for scraping leads. 

We use 3-4 softwares and keep adding new ones.

We have a person who manually scrapes a few things like websites, communities and a few other portals. 

Then we enrich and send emails.

2. Using personalisation with relevancy:

Most people think that they can just mention a random life event they posted about and think that their prospects will respond.

Example:

Hey x, saw you guys went to a boat party with your team hope you had fun now here’s what im selling…..

This may not work! ❌

Here’s what we do:

✅ Personalisation only when the subject is relevant

Example: hey x, you commented on my post that x is an issue for you guys! What if I can help you solve x in y weeks? I’m only saying this because we have helped clients like a,b,c solve the same problem!

I would love to send you more information on this you can send it to your team as well. Let me know!

Now if you see the second example has relevancy on to why we are reaching out.

3. Having private webinars:

Instead of asking them for a call.

We reached out to them saying that we are hosting a private webinar with only 5 participants.

This makes them feel special plus we don't have to jump on 5 separate calls in one hour. 

We explain the funnel to those 5 participants with a crazy upsell and downsell offer then we proceed if they are interested.

And that’s what!

We keep changing our funnel every month. 

Let me know your thoughts here! 

#sales #leadgeneration #coldemail

7 dark truths about lead gen 👇🏻

	1.	The most qualified buyers don’t engage with your posts.

They screenshot, share in Slack, and show up in your Stripe.

	2.	If your offer isn’t instantly understood by a 10-year-old, it’s too weak to scale.

Confusion kills faster than competition.

	3.	Great outreach doesn’t feel personalized. It feels psychic.

“Wait… how did they know I was dealing with this?” — that’s when they reply.

	4.	You don’t need a new tool. 

You need a better reason for people to care.

No AI, no automation can save a boring offer.

	5.	Your brand is just how people describe you when you’re not in the room.

If it’s not triggering emotion, it’s forgettable.

	6.	You think you need volume. 

You actually need momentum.

3 right leads per week with intent > 100 uninterested clicks.

	7.	If you’re not polarizing, you’re invisible.

Being liked by everyone is the fastest way to be ignored by buyers.

[POST] 2025-04-12 | activity 7316800741719838720 | likes 796 | comments 343 | shares 26
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7316800741719838720

You can build this AI SDR this weekend and it can start running your lead gen campaigns Monday onwards (full process ⬇️)

With the rise of AI agents we have started pivoting towards agents instead of relying on $1000/mo tech stack. 

You can too.

This AI SDR:

→ Finds leads
→ Enriches their data
→ Writes cold emails
→ Pushes them into campaigns
→ Notifies the team

All on autopilot.

Here’s the full workflow you can build today—

Step 1: Capture Leads Using Google Search API + n8n 

Goal: Scrape LinkedIn URLs for people in your niche.

Tools needed:

→ Google Custom Search API (free tier available)
→ N8N (open source automation platform)
→ Google Sheets

Example ICP:

“SaaS founders in New York with <50 employees”

Steps:
	1.	Set up Google Programmable Search Engine (CSE)
	2.	Get the API key + CSE ID
	3.	In N8N:
	•	Add an HTTP module to call the search API
	•	Use a Loop module to paginate (get 100+ results)
	•	Add a WAIT function (1s delay) to avoid rate limits
	•	Send all LinkedIn URLs to Google Sheets (via Google Sheets module)

⸻

Step 2: Enrich Leads with Company + Contact Info

Goal: Turn raw URLs into full contact data.

Tools needed:
→ Proxycurl (for company & title)
→ Dropcontact (for email & phone)
→ Google Sheets
→ N8N

Steps:
	1.	In N8N, take each LinkedIn URL
	2.	Call Proxycurl API:
	•	Extract: Name, Title, Company, Company Website
	3.	Then call Dropcontact API:
	•	Extract: Verified Email, Phone, LinkedIn handle

Pro Tip: Use “IF” condition in N8N to skip blank responses.

⸻

Step 3: Qualify Leads Using GPT-4

Goal: Use AI to decide if this lead is worth emailing.

How to do it:

1.	Create a prompt like:

“Here’s my ICP: Founder of a SaaS company with <$10M revenue, hiring SDRs. Does this person match?”

2.	Feed enriched lead data into GPT-4

3.	GPT returns:

{
  "lead_potential": "High",
  "lead_score": 8.9
}

4.	Store this output back into Google Sheets.

Step 4: Generate Personalized Ice Breakers (GPT-4)

Goal: Make your emails sound human—not robotic.

Prompt Example:
“Write a 1-line intro based on this landing page. Highlight something unique, like their mission or design.”

Output Example:
“Loved how you framed your product as the ‘Grammarly for SDRs’—super crisp.”

Paste this icebreaker into the “first line” column in your Sheet.

⸻

Step 5: Auto-Send via Smartlead  or HeyReach.io 

Goal: Send emails or LinkedIn messages without lifting a finger.

Steps:

	•	Only include pages with matching keywords in title

[RESHARE] 2025-04-19 | activity 7319222863318679552 | likes 1191 | comments 95 | shares 148
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7319222863318679552
(Reshared from: Manthan Patel)

2025 is the Year of ACP, not just MCP.

IBM has introduced a new protocol for AI collaboration called Agent Communication Protocol, building upon the foundation laid by Anthropic's Model Context Protocol.

ACP takes a leap forward in how AI systems work together, allowing complex multi-agent workflows that were impossible with MCP alone.

Here's how ACP works:
1️⃣ Agent Orchestration
ACP enables multiple AI agents to communicate seamlessly, allowing specialized agents to combine their capabilities.

2️⃣ Standardized Messaging
The protocol uses structured message formats that help agents understand each other across different frameworks and languages.

3️⃣ Task Delegation
Complex problems are broken down and assigned to the most capable specialized agents, then results are assembled into cohesive solutions.

4️⃣ Framework Independence
ACP works with agents built in any programming language or AI framework, removing technical barriers to collaboration.

5️⃣ Dynamic Discovery
Agents can discover and utilize each other's capabilities, creating flexible AI ecosystems that evolve to meet changing needs.

Whether you're building complex AI workflows or connecting specialized agents, ACP elevates what's possible, enabling deeper collaboration and more powerful solutions.

Here's how ACP is architecturally different from MCP:

MCP:
- Focuses on connecting a single AI to external data sources and tools
- Creates one-to-many relationships between an AI and various resources
- Uses JSON-RPC primarily for accessing information and executing actions
- Designed to expand what one AI model can access and accomplish

ACP:
- Centers on connecting multiple AIs to each other in collaborative relationships
- Creates many-to-many networks of specialized agent capabilities
- Extends JSON-RPC with agent-specific communication patterns
- Designed for dividing complex tasks among specialized AI team members

Understanding these distinctions matters for building the right AI infrastructure. Some problems need better tools for one AI. Others need multiple AIs working together.

ACP isn't just different from MCP; it's complementary:

✅ Solves problems too complex for any single AI agent
✅ Creates AI teams with specialized members handling different aspects of a task
✅ Enables more natural workflows that mirror human team collaboration

The combination of MCP and ACP is essential. MCP gives individual AIs access to tools and data. ACP helps those AIs work together as teams. Together, they create AI systems that are more capable, flexible, and effective.

Over to you: What complex problems could you solve with a team of specialized AI agents working together?

We built an AI agent that turns LinkedIn URLs into qualified leads.

Here’s how it works (built inside n8n):

1. Trigger

→ Feed in a LinkedIn profile URL via webhook or schedule.

2. URL Parser

→ Extracts and verifies the profile link.

3. LinkedIn Scraper

→ Pulls data like name, title, company, industry, about section, etc.

4. Data Cleanup

→ Removes noise, structures the info, and gets it AI-ready.

5. Prompt Builder

→ Merges the scraped data with a clear task (e.g., “Write a cold email” or “Summarize this prospect’s pain points”).

6. Claude / GPT

→ Generates highly personalized outreach copy based on real profile data.

7. Output Handler

→ Formats the response and auto-sends it to Slack, Email, Sheets, or Notion.

8. Optional Enrichment

→ Tags prospect by ICP fit, score, and engagement potential (fully automated).
