[POST] 2025-04-22 | activity 7320496262586245121 | likes 257 | comments 51 | shares 9
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7320496262586245121

We built an AI agent that turns a LinkedIn profile into a LEAD copy my full process)

- 100% Automated 
- No Extensions
- No Manual Scraping

All you need to do is paste a LinkedIn search link that you want to scrape and this agent figures out..

Scrapes every LinkedIn URL, enriches the data, and builds a complete lead sheet while you're busy doing… literally anything else.

Here’s how you can build it for yourself this week: 

Step 1: Upload LI profile URLs or a Google sheet

- Tools: Google Sheet API
- Go to Google Cloud Console
- Make a new project and a credential
- Install the Google Drive and Google Sheets API

In n8n:
 
- Add a new credential for Google Sheet using the Client ID and Client Secret
- Add a "Chat Trigger" node
- Connect it to an OpenAI module to determine the nature of input
- Connect it to an IF node: If the above output is "LinkedIn", then execute the 1st workflow, if it's google sheets, then run the 2nd workflow.

-----------------------------------
Step 2: Extracting The Links

Now, let's extract the LinkedIn URLs from the Chat input.
In N8n:
- Connect the "True" endpoint of the last IF node to a Code node
- Use ChatGPT or Claude AI to generate the code
- Connect it to a "Split out" node and connect the "Split out" node to "Loop Over Items" node
-----------------------------------

Step 3: Enriching the LinkedIn URLs
In N8n:

- Connect the "Loop Over Items" node to a HTTP node and use APify's LinkedIn profile scraping API to enrich the profiles with basic data
- Do the same for scraping the company profiles using Apify's LI company scraping APIs

-----------------------------------

Step 4: Scraping the business website
In N8n:

- Use an HTTP node and Apify's web scraping API to scrape the entire website
- Use an "OpenAI Node" after this to format the scraped data

-----------------------------------

Step 5: Scraping the email id
In N8n:

- Download the Icypeas node from the community option
- Create the credential using your Icypeas API
- Connect the last openai node to the Icypeas node and format the icypeas node to find the email
- Add a wait node of 3-5 Seconds
- Connect it to a HTTP node using Icypeas documentation to get the scraped emails

-----------------------------------

Step 6: Organize everything into a Google Sheet

- Connect a SET node to the "Done" endpoint of the "Loop Over items" node and clean the data
- Add an aggregate node
- Connect a Google sheet node and format it to create a new Google Sheet
- Then use a code node to convert the aggregate data
- Add another SET node and clean the data
- Connect it to a Google sheet node and format it for "Append rows"

You could use this to: 

→ Book qualified calls
→ Build retargeting lists
→ Feed outbound workflows
→ Warm up cold audiences
→ Feed into Smartlead or Clay

This AI agent can automate 80% of your lead generation.

Today’s Day 2 of building the "Lead Magnet Automation Agent."

Yesterday, you scraped the lead's personal and company profile details.

Today, you’ll:
- Scrape their business website
- Prompt the AI agent to qualify leads
- Scrape and verify their emails

Step 1: Business website scraping
- Add another HTTP node  
- Use Apify’s API and add the details in the HTTP node  
- Add an OpenAI node and prompt it to format the scraped data

------------------------------------------

Step 2: AI lead qualification
- Add an AI module  
- Attach the OpenAI chat model  
- In the system prompt, use:  
"You are an AI agent that qualifies prospects based on their personal and company details.  
Analyze the provided input about a person and their company and determine qualification according to these rules:  
1. The company’s location must be in the USA, UK, Canada, or Australia.  
2. The company must be a SaaS business in the lead generation, marketing, or sales industry.  

Output Rules:  
1. If both criteria match, output only: 'Qualified'  
2. If the business type matches but the location does not, output only: 'Qualified'  
3. If neither matches, output only: 'Unqualified'  
4. Do not include any extra explanation or formatting."

- Change the criteria as needed  
- Map the company location and formatted data in the user prompt

------------------------------------------

Step 3: Route
- Add an IF node  
- Set the condition: Previous output is "Qualified"

------------------------------------------

Step 4: Email scraping and verification
- Connect an HTTP node to the TRUE branch of the IF node  
- Connect Icypeas API  
- Map the relevant data  
- Add a 5 second WAIT node  
- Add another HTTP node to fetch the email  
- Add an HTTP node with Million Verifier's API to verify the emails

You’ve now completed 80% of the work.

- Paste the "Update message sequence" CURL command from Smartlead
- Make the changes in the settings

Last week our agent stole customers from a huge saas company here’s what we did:

We built a Tech Stack Finder Agent that tells us what tools a company uses.

This agent replaces software like BuiltWith that costs $995/mo.

🧠 The Workflow — Step-by-Step:

1. Input: Company Name

→ Pulled directly from scraped posts, LinkedIn comments, or Google Sheets
 → Sent to n8n

2. Tech Stack Detection

→ Called Proxycurl’s company API
 → Got: Tech stack, size, industry, revenue, LinkedIn URL

3. Use GPT to Qualify
Prompt:

“Check if this company uses [Apollo, Outreach, Hubspot]. If yes, return relevance score + reason.”
Output:
json
CopyEdit
{
  "score": 9.3,
  "reason": "Uses Apollo. Their team fits our ICP."
}


4. Sort & Tag

→ If score ≥ 8, tag as “Tech-Fit”
 → Auto-skip the rest

→ Smartlead sequences by tool ( {competitor users} → teardown)

We sent 1M+ cold emails last year. 80% of replies came from 3 things no one on LinkedIn talks about.

(And no it’s not your subject line, icebreaker, or GPT prompt.)

⸻

Cold email doesn’t die. 

Founders just get bored of doing the boring stuff that works.

We sent 1M+ cold emails last year.

Across different industries. Different price points.

Here’s what actually works (and what’ll probably still work 5 years from now):

⸻

1. Nobody cares about your offer if they don’t trust your inbox.

Most cold email advice skips this:

If your domain is dusty, your emails are invisible.
We test nothing if open rates drop below 30%.

- We don’t fix subject lines.
- We don’t rewrite hooks.
- We change the damn domain.
And guess what? It works.

⸻

2. Don’t follow up 6 times. Rebuild.

You can keep sending the same sequence with a new signature.

Or you can stop, rewrite the offer, and send again two months later.

The internet doesn’t remember you.
But your timing might hit differently.

⸻

3. Everyone is trying to sell. We just ask the right question.

Our best email last year didn’t even have a CTA.

It was literally:

“You hired 2 SDRs in 30 days. Curious what’s the plan if they don’t ramp fast enough?”

That was it.

Smart people reply to smart observations.

⸻

4. You’re not getting ghosted because of your copy. You’re getting ghosted because your email looks like a pitch.

If it looks like a campaign, smells like a sequence, and has 4 links in it it’s spam.

We stopped personalizing intros. We started referencing decisions.

	•	Hiring
	•	Tools removed
	•	Job descriptions updated

That’s what gets replies now.

⸻

5. Everyone’s scared to reuse the same leads. We recycle them like steel.

We run the same lists every quarter with new angles.

Because pain changes faster than people.
If Q1 was a “not now,” Q2 might be “I’m listening.”

⸻

This isn’t a thread. It’s just what I know.

After building campaigns that bombed.

And then building ones that turned strangers into clients within 48 hours.

⸻

Cold email hasn’t changed. 

People just keep skipping the part where they’re supposed to think.

If you’re tired of templates and want a system that works like a founder’s brain,

Yesterday my co founder and my account got hacked. Here’s what happened (and how we got them back):

Even our company page, AcquisitionX was banned. (It’s still under shadow ban) 

Whoever hacked was trying to run ads from our page we have never ran any ads but we saw our ads account was active. 

LinkedIn suspected it and restricted our accounts.

But here’s the scary part:

Before this even happened, hundreds of people in our network started receiving emails and WhatsApp messages from random numbers pretending to be us.

They used our names, our company’s name.

- Some messages included links. 
- Some asked for calls.
- It was a targeted impersonation attack.

We ignored the early signs. 

We thought it was spam. But it was part of a bigger breach attempt.

Last month, one of our team members also got banned. 

We only got her account back after publicly posting on LinkedIn.

Right now, our page is still shadowbanned you won’t find it if you search. 

Here’s how you can get your account back if it’s restricted:

- Change your password immediately
- Log out of all sessions
- If you don’t hear back from linkedin keep raising tickets even if you get “no” as an ans
- Report to LinkedIn Support with proof
- Post publicly + tag @LinkedInHelp from peer help
(It speeds things up trust me)
- Avoid third party tools 
- If your chrome has a lot of extensions added dont use LinkedIn from that browser 

Go multichannel and never stop building your email list. 

That’s all! 

If anyone need any help with LinkedIn acc restriction feel free to ask me. 

If you’re reading this, please engage (like, comment, or share).

It helps signal to LinkedIn that we’re real people.

(save it for future) 

—Suprava
Co-founder, AcquisitionX

[POST] 2025-06-03 | activity 7335696767285407744 | likes 85 | comments 44 | shares 2
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7335696767285407744

These cold DM frameworks will give you 50%+ response rate. (Tried and tested) 

Your cold DMs get ignored because they feel like outreach.

Here’s how to win them over with just DMs. 5 cold DM frameworks that consistently get replies —

1. Unsolicited Insight:

“Saw your post on [topic].
I’ve been tracking how 3 similar companies are approaching that differently.

If you’re into positioning experiments, happy to send over what I found.”

Why it works:

You’re not asking for time. You’re sharing something useful no strings attached.

Use GPT + LinkedIn scraper + competitor site scraper to analyze how others position the same product or problem.

2. Voice Note with Context:

[20-second voice note]

“Today’s a bit chilly haha the reason I reached out because I just saw (pain point) that might help with [problem] you mentioned.
Lemme know if you want me to send. ”

You can:

Set up a listener that flags when a prospect posts or comments on a relevant topic → 

triggers a reminder for you to send a manual note.

3. Time-Lag Follow-Up:

“You commented on a post about [topic] a few months ago.
I wanna check if anything shifted since then [pain point]?”

Why it works:

You’re not pitching you’re continuing a thought they had before.

Log engagements across your CRM, timestamp them, and build a 30/60/90-day re-engagement flow based on topic tags.

4. Mutual Activity Signal

“Saw your name in a few places
Looks like [problem] is showing up across a few teams in your space.

how you’re approaching it on your end?”

You can cross-reference connections, mutual comments, or tool usage overlap to triangulate when 2+ people in a niche group are discussing the same problem.

5. Permission-Based Pitch

“I help [ICP] with [specific pain] —
But I’ll ask directly: are you even open to hearing a pitch if the timing and relevance are right?”

Why it works:

It’s honest. No baiting, no pretending.

What ties all of these together?

- Relevance over personalization

- Curiosity over persuasion

- Timing over templates

- Signal over noise

Cold outreach isn’t dead.
Generic outreach is.

If you want the DM templates, agent setups to track such signals, and a Notion doc on how to trigger these without sounding like a bot then let me know below and I’ll send it over.

[POST] 2025-06-04 | activity 7336048890468560896 | likes 180 | comments 75 | shares 10
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7336048890468560896

You can build this AI SDR this weekend and it can start running your lead gen campaigns Monday onwards (full process ⬇️)

With the rise of AI agents we have started pivoting towards agents instead of relying on $1000/mo tech stack. 

You can too.

This AI SDR:

→ Finds leads
→ Enriches their data
→ Writes cold emails
→ Pushes them into campaigns
→ Notifies the team

All on autopilot.

Here’s the full workflow you can build today—

Step 1: Capture Leads Using Google Search API + n8n  

Goal: Scrape LinkedIn URLs for people in your niche.

Tools needed:

→ Google Custom Search API (free tier available)
→ N8N (open source automation platform)
→ Google Sheets

Example ICP:

“SaaS founders in New York with <50 employees”

Steps:
	1.	Set up Google Programmable Search Engine (CSE)
	2.	Get the API key + CSE ID
	3.	In N8N:
	•	Add an HTTP module to call the search API
	•	Use a Loop module to paginate (get 100+ results)
	•	Add a WAIT function (1s delay) to avoid rate limits
	•	Send all LinkedIn URLs to Google Sheets (via Google Sheets module)

⸻

Step 2: Enrich Leads with Company + Contact Info

Goal: Turn raw URLs into full contact data.

Tools needed:
→ Proxycurl (for company & title)
→ Dropcontact (for email & phone)
→ Google Sheets
→ N8N

Steps:

1.	In N8N, take each LinkedIn URL
2.	Call Proxycurl API:
Extract: Name, Title, Company, Company Website
3.	Then call Dropcontact API:
Extract: Verified Email, Phone, LinkedIn handle

Pro Tip: Use “IF” condition in N8N to skip blank responses.

⸻

Step 3: Qualify Leads Using GPT-4

Goal: Use AI to decide if this lead is worth emailing.

How to do it:

1.	Create a prompt like:

“Here’s my ICP: Founder of a SaaS company with <$10M revenue, hiring SDRs. Does this person match?”

2.	Feed enriched lead data into GPT-4

3.	GPT returns:

{
  "lead_potential": "High",
  "lead_score": 8.9
}

4.	Store this output back into Google Sheets.

Step 4: Generate Personalized Ice Breakers (GPT-4)

Goal: Make your emails sound human—not robotic.

Prompt Example:

“Write a 1-line intro based on this landing page. Highlight something unique, like their mission or design.”

Output Example:

“Loved how you framed your product as the ‘Grammarly for SDRs’—super crisp.”

Paste this icebreaker into the “first line” column in your Sheet.

⸻

Step 5: Auto-Send via Smartlead or HeyReach.io  

Goal: Send emails or LinkedIn messages without lifting a finger.

Steps:

	1.	In N8N, add HTTP module
	2.	Use Smartlead/Heyreach API
	3.	Push lead info + campaign ID
	4.	Auto-mark “YES” in your Sheet once sent

Step 6: Live Notifications in Slack

Goal: Keep your team updated in real time.

Tools:
→ Slack
→ N8N

———

And that’s it! 

Thanks to Manthan who built this for us. 

If you’re looking to try this SDR lemme know! Will send you more info. :)

Clay won’t save your business neither AI agents..

I’ve been getting on a lot of calls lately, and prospects keep asking:

“If I use this AI agent… will I get 30–50 booked calls a month?” 🤥

“We’re already paying $3K/month on tools… can you build an agent that does all this for free?”🤥

“I’m hearing a lot about clay can we hit quota if we use clay?” 🤥

Let me be real with you:

Clay is great

AI agents can help you automate better.

But they won’t fix the real problem.

Clay won’t fix a weak offer.

AI agents won’t fix unclear messaging.

And automation won’t fix poor targeting.

Also, AI agents aren’t free.

- You still need APIs.
- You still need Apify.

These setups take time, money, and actual thinking.

They don’t magically work just because you connect a few tools.

Now let’s talk about the real issue:

Intent.

If you’re reaching out to people who don’t actually care, no tool in the world is going to get you results.

Here’s what intent looks like:

- Someone visited your site multiple times
- Just hired a new head of sales or marketing
- Job post live for 30+ days with no hire
- Founder suddenly active on LinkedIn again
- Launching something but no engagemen
- They just raised funding (pls dont reach out as soon as they raise funds wait atleast for a while reach with value)
- They’re already using a tool you can replace

That’s intent. 

That’s when outreach works.

Everything else is guessing.

If your direction is wrong, it only gets you lost faster.

[POST] 2025-06-12 | activity 7338967691891736579 | likes 194 | comments 41 | shares 9
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7338967691891736579

We scraped 50K+ high-intent leads with reverse engineering method. Here’s how: 

Step 1: Find Events Where Your Ideal Customers Already Gather

Go to LinkedIn → Search → Filter by Events

Type in:

	•	“B2B SaaS Founder Webinar”
	•	“AI Product Demo”
	•	“Marketing Masterclass”

Filter for:

	•	Events with 500+ attendees
	•	Recent or upcoming dates
	•	Relevant titles (Founder, CEO, VP of Marketing)

Example:

The event “AI Prompting Chat” hosted by Allie K. Miller had 6.8K attendees, mostly tech founders and AI builders.

👻 Step 2: Scrape Attendees Using PhantomBuster

Tool: PhantomBuster  – LinkedIn Event Guest Extractor

What to do:

	•	Paste the event URL
	•	Set scrape limits (we did 1,000–5,000 attendees per event)
	•	Export the data

What you’ll get:
	•	Full Name
	•	LinkedIn Profile URL
	•	Job Title
	•	Company
	•	Location

Repeat for 10–15 events and you’ll hit 50K+ profiles in days.


Step 3: Clean and Filter

We import the CSVs into Google Sheets or Airtable

Then:

❌ Remove:

- Students, interns
- People without job titles
- Irrelevant countries (keep US, UK, CA, AU)

✅ Keep:

- Titles like: Founder, CEO, Head of Growth
- ICP-matching roles

What you now have = a refined pool of active decision-makers who voluntarily RSVP’d to an event in your niche.

Step 4: Enrich With Verified Emails

Tool: Findymail (or any leadfinder you prefer)

Upload the cleaned list.

You’ll get:

-30–60% verified business emails
-Export-ready enriched CSV

🔥Pro tip: The cleaner your original list, the higher the match rate.


Step 5: Use the Leads

Now choose your outbound method:

A) Upload to Smartlead → Cold email
B) Use LinkedIn DMs (manual or via PhantomBuster)
C) Feed into your CRM or HubSpot  sequences

This pipeline generated booked calls within 48 hours. Zero ad spend.

Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7350761248583495681

What if you could see your thoughts as a neural map?

Imagine visualizing your entire ChatGPT conversation history as a 3D web of interconnected ideas.

Here’s how that could be done:

	1.	Export the chat history from ChatGPT settings
	2.	Clean the messages to isolate the raw conversations
	3.	Generate embeddings using an AI model like text-embedding-3-small
	4.	Measure similarity between conversations using cosine distance
	5.	Build a graph with similar thoughts linked
	6.	Visualize it in 3D using tools like Gephi, Graphistry, or 3d-force-graph.js

The result:

A living, breathing map of one’s thought patterns — clustered by topics, connected by curiosity, and layered with personal evolution.

The next frontier might not be AI doing the thinking for us…

…but showing us how we think.

Thoughts on this?

LinkedIn outreach got our clients over $320K in pipeline last quarter and we never touched their personal profiles.. (full process)

We sent 3000+ inmails every single month for one of the client 

The client had broad TAM so it worked really well. 

We didn’t use their personal LinkedIn account…

Because here’s the truth:

Most people do LinkedIn outreach backwards.

They chase leads from the wrong profile, burn their personal brand, and blame the channel.

We flipped the model.

Here’s our exact process:

1. Warm-up aged LinkedIn accounts

→ Get verified profiles with clean track records
→ No spam history = no risk of bans

2. Rebrand them to match the ICP

→ Think: “Growth Partner for B2B SaaS” or “Outbound Advisor for Agencies”
→ Positioning is built to mirror the buyer, not push a founder

3. Publish 2–3 lead magnets per week with workflows 

→ Swipe files, ROI calculators, teardown carousels
→ Content that actually solves problems

4. Capture interest → funnel into warm emails

Every commenter/DM goes into a short nurture funnel

→ Day 1: Asset
→ Day 3: How to use it
→ Day 6: Soft invite to chat

5. Add automation to scale the backend

→ AI qualifies replies
→ SDRs handle follow-ups
→ Founder only steps in for high-fit convos

6. Scale with InMails

→ For one client, we’re sending 3,000 InMails/month

→ Booking 50–100 qualified calls — at a lower CAC than paid ads

This system works because the founder stays out of the noise.

Your job isn’t to chase leads 

It’s to build leverage and stay rare.

Let the system sell.

Let you stay strategic.

Our AI SDR system generates $70K every month. Here’s how it works

Oh and it beats even best reps without damaging the brand.

Here’s exactly how we rebuilt our SDR motion step by step:

1. We don’t use our real LinkedIn profiles for outreach.

It’s tempting to use your own face.

But the truth is if you send more than you post, your account slowly dies.

So we build avatar accounts instead.

They post lead magnets that actually bring leads.

Then we keep a goal of sending 3K+ InMails a month 

We focus on volume.

It’s safer, scalable, and works better long term.

We automate the DMs but in a way that doesn’t spam here HeyReach.io helps us.

2. Started tracking signals:

We stopped buying databases.

Every lead now enters the funnel based on a trigger:

- Visited our site anonymously
- Engaged with a competitor
- Posted a relevant job
- Raised a funding round
- Started hiring rapidly

That one shift from static to signal changed everything.

Riddhiman helped us make ai agents that tracks these.

You can check Trigify.io for this too.

3. Shifted to prompts from templates:

No "Hope you're well."
No “quick question.”

Each message is generated based on the context:

 → What’s the goal?
 → Who’s the lead and what triggered them?
 → What’s the tone that’ll land best?
 → What’s already been said?

We built a custom GPT that writes it for us.

4. We only chase leads worth chasing.

 AI agent monitors behavior in real time:

 → Email opens, click depth
 → LinkedIn activity
 → CRM movement

High-intent leads get fast-tracked.

Everyone else either gets nurtured… or ignored.

That alone gave us 30% more bandwidth with no performance drop.

Breakcold is pretty solid when it comes to CRM tracking.

5. Conversations happen. Then AI qualifies.
 When someone replies:

First-layer questions are handled automatically

BANT-style qualification runs in the background

Calendars get booked if it’s a fit

Nuanced replies get routed to a human

And when video or voice adds context, we use it only then.

6. Follow-ups switch channels based on behavior.

 If someone ignores the first message, we don’t resend it louder.

We shift to another platform:

 Email → LinkedIn
 LinkedIn → Twitter (sometimes)

 …and sometimes back to email with a new angle.

Smartlead is the goat when it comes to email 

We separated identity from infrastructure.

We now run outbound through fully warmed, pre-aged profiles not our personal ones.

They act like humans

They’re safe to automate

And if something happens?

 Our real brand stays untouched.

We still post.

This system helped us scale faster.

[POST] 2025-07-28 | activity 7355546504767463424 | likes 158 | comments 35 | shares 6
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7355546504767463424

Google is deleting your cold emails before they even hit inbox. 

This happened to us recently!!

We had:

- Clean list.
- Warmed domain.
- SPF, DKIM, DMARC — all ✅
- Great copy.

But stil…

Zero replies.

We thought something was off.

It was: our infrastructure.

Gmail doesn’t just scan your words.
It scans how you send, from where, and what patterns you follow.

If any of it looks off, your emails are erased before they reach anyone.

What fixed it for us:

1. We set-up our own Infra:

We set up our own SMTP server Postfix on a VPS.

Hooked it to a dedicated IP.

No shared infra. 

No third-party fingerprints.

→ Clean sender identity.

Now Gmail treats us as a standalone sender not a bulk app user.

2. We fully configured DNS (beyond the basics)

Most people set SPF, DKIM, and DMARC and think they’re done.

Wrong.

You also need:

- PTR records (reverse DNS)
- FCrDNS (forward-confirmed reverse DNS)

These are what Gmail uses to verify that your IP = your domain.

3. We slowed everything down

Started sending 5–10 plain-text emails/day.

Just real messages, written manually.

Replies boosted sender reputation.

Then we scaled up slowly over 45 days.

Volume only grew when reply rates held steady.

4. We re-learned how to write

No fancy formatting.
No AI-sounding “quick questions.”
No calendar links.

Instead we used:

- all small caps email
- made mistakes on emails

Gmail flags anything that looks templated or mass-produced.

5. We implemented greylisting + rate-limiting

Most people don’t know what this is.

We configured our server to:

Accept retry attempts from legit mail servers

The bursts that look like spam behavior

Gmail tracks this. 

It’s their way of filtering real senders from spam bots.

If your infra can’t retry or slows down under load that’s a good thing.

6. We started live monitoring everything

We now use internal dashboards to monitor:

- Spam feedback loops (Spamcop, AOL, etc.)
- IP reputation health
- Open/reply/soft bounce signals
- Domain temperature

Auto-rotation of sending IPs

One red flag…

The system pauses, reroutes, and resets warm-up if needed.

Most founders optimize copy.
Smart founders fix infrastructure.

Google’s AI doesn’t care how clever your hook is.

It cares how trustworthy your server looks from the outside.

I’m still learning and implementing things day by day 

——

Send this to your SDR

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[POST] 2025-07-30 | activity 7356294324017364992 | likes 104 | comments 32 | shares 6
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7356294324017364992

If your cold emails are getting 0 replies high chances AI is deleting your emails.

Gmail’s AI now looks at how you send not just what you send.

We learned this the hard way.

Here are 11 deliverability signals you need to watch for:

1. Dedicated SMTP on VPS

We ditched shared sending tools.
Set up Postfix on our own VPS with a dedicated IP.

→ Gmail saw a clean sender identity.
→ No third-party footprints.
→ Inbox rate jumped.

2. Full DNS config (beyond the basics)

Everyone sets up SPF, DKIM, DMARC.
But that’s not enough.

We added:

- PTR records (reverse DNS)
- FCrDNS (forward-confirmed reverse DNS)

These verify that your IP truly belongs to your domain. Gmail checks this.

3. Manual warm-up, slow scaling

We started by sending just 5–10 plain-text emails/day.

No links. No HTML. No tools. Just… real emails.

Once reply rates held steady → we scaled gradually over 45 days.

4. Rate-limiting + greylisting config

We made our infrastructure mimic human behavior:

- Slow response to bursts
- Accept retries from real mail servers

→ Gmail uses this to separate humans from bots.

5. Live monitoring dashboard

We now track:

- Spam complaint loops (via Spamcop, AOL, etc.)
- IP and domain reputation
- Open / reply/bounce patterns
- Domain “temperature” by ESP

⚠ One red flag → system pauses, reroutes, resets warm-up.

6. IP rotation logic

When one IP gets hot or flagged → we rotate.
Reputation protected. Deliverability stable.

7. Consistent send windows

No 2 AM email blasts.
We trained Gmail to expect behavior that looks… human.

8. Inbox placement testing

We test inbox placement daily across Gmail, Outlook, Zoho, Proton, and more.

It’s the only way to catch silent spam filtering.

9. Clean, human copy

No AI polish. No “quick question” openers.
We used lowercase subjects, occasional typos, and a human-like tone.

Gmail flags anything that looks like a mass template.

10. Avoided known spam triggers

We stripped:

- Link shorteners
- Scheduling tools
- Spammy CTAs

Gmail flags predictable phrases fast.

11. Used Warmy.io - Email channel. Reliable. to keep everything stable

We now run Warmy alongside our infrastructure.

It detects deliverability risks → AI crafts optimized warmup sequences

- Auto-warmup across 150K emails/month in 30+ languages
- Human takes over once inbox placement stabilizes

It’s the quiet system in the background constantly making sure we stay clean, warm, and consistent.

Google doesn’t care how clever your hook is.
It cares if your server looks is clean.

⸻

Send this to your SDR before they write the next campaign.

3️⃣ Switch subject lines and preview text every batch.
4️⃣ Stagger send times never dump at the top of the hour.

6️⃣ Purge graymail weekly dead weight kills deliverability over time.
7️⃣ Run a “zero-link” day each week to lift domain trust.
8️⃣ Use two tracking domains per sequencer and alternate daily.

[POST] 2025-08-25 | activity 7365707553420247041 | likes 75 | comments 25 | shares 5
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7365707553420247041

This cold email framework has a 70% reply rate 👇

And the craziest part?

Most marketers still don’t know about it.

It’s in the subject line…

Stop writing subject lines like 

“quick thoughts?” 

or mentioning their company name.

It feels very spammy.

And honestly pls stop using fake compliments 🙏

Instead, try this:

Use “Re: [meeting]” or any 2–3 word phrase that feels natural.

Your subject line is 90% of the email game.

And here the “re” looks like a reply 

Rather than an email

Then in the email:

1. Open with something that will hook them

2. Mention names of clients in similar niche of them if not just leave it be.

3. Introduce yourself in one line. (Yeah it still works) 

4. In the CTA mention you would like to send them their favourite food if they hop on a call with you. 

Or say you would like to pay for their time 

The wild part?

Almost no one takes the money.

But they do take the call.

——

This has worked for me multiple times.

Try it today!

[POST] 2025-08-26 | activity 7366000284214435841 | likes 60 | comments 24 | shares 0
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7366000284214435841

This outreach mistake is killing 90% of your sales calls

If you book meetings through outreach…

The WORST thing you can do is jump on the call and ask discovery questions.

Your prospects don’t want to teach you about their problems. 

They want you to already know. 

Even if you’re doing mass outreach, here’s how to make it better: 👇


1. Segment properly:

Don’t just target “SaaS.” 

Break it down. 

Within SaaS you have micro-industries — 

-AI tools
-sales tech
-marketing platforms, etc. 

Then go deeper: what specific pain points exist in each micro-niche?

Upload those lists into ChatGPT and ask it to map out the likely problems. 

This way your outreach speaks directly to their reality.


2. Create assets that add value:

Skip the generic case study PDF. Instead, make lightweight, useful assets that show you’ve done your homework:

- Guides work really well 
- Funnel walkthroughs or mind maps using Whimsical.
- Short explainer videos/guides using Gamma or Tella.

These position you as a problem-solver rather than just another salesperson.


3. Warm them up before the call:

Use Feather (YC S22) to do a check-in call.

It can even clone your voice.

Prospect feels a personal touch 
→ you save time.

4. On the actual call:

The first 10 minutes should feel conversational, not like an interrogation. 

Frame your insights as observations and let them confirm or correct you. 

For example:

1. From our data and similar clients, we noticed X is a recurring challenge in your industry. Is that something you’re running into as well?”

2. “We figured out Y approach for a client in your space that drove Z results. Would you be open if we applied a similar playbook here?”

3. Close by aligning: “If this direction makes sense, we’ll map the exact steps so you can see what it would look like for you.”

Even if you run mass outreach…

The call feels personal.

And they think: this person already gets me.

——

I follow this framework :))

I’ve handled over 1000+ clients now and these are some common lead gen mistakes most make and how you can avoid 👇🏻

1. Thinking outreach is only quality. 

Outreach is a volume game. 

Even the best copy fails if you’re only sending 100 emails a week.

2. Thinking only outreach is enough.

It’s 2025. 

Buyers need to see you everywhere: 

LinkedIn, email, YouTube, webinars, even offline.

3. Believing content is a long-term game.

Not true. 

With the right funnel, 

Content drives faster leads than outreach.

4. A great tool stack will fix my funnel.

Tools only amplify what’s already working. 

They don’t create demand.

5. Thinking cold email gives instant results.

In reality, the first month is warming up, 

The next few weeks are testing..

And results come after 2 months.

7. Hiring an agency will save my business.

Don’t hire an agency if you don’t already have a sales team. 

Or have tried different marketing approach 

First try things on your own or with a team. 

Otherwise it’s only going to waste your money.

——

These are some common issues I see everyday. 

Adding some more in the comments 👇🏻

LinkedIn just took down Amplemarket’s page. (A $12M+ company)

Your entire brand can vanish in a single day.

If your product is built on someone else’s data without permission, you don’t own your business. They do.

And it’s not just Amplemarket.

1. Apollo lost access earlier this year.

2. Seamless faced the same issue.

3. Proxycurl was sued into shutting down.

But there are ways you can protect yourself here are a few:

1. Build a newsletter pipeline: own your audience, don’t rent it. 

An email list is the one channel nobody can take away from you.

2. Create AI avatars: so even if accounts go down, your voice and outreach stay alive across channels.

3. Leverage team profiles for content: don’t put everything on one founder account. Distribute content across multiple trusted voices.

4. Spread multichannel: tutorials, case studies, playbooks, courses, Youtube, X everywhere that you can think of.

The future isn’t about chasing hacks.

It’s about building resilient systems that survive platform shifts.

Because whether it’s LinkedIn, email, or ads the rules can change overnight.

And the companies that win will be the ones that planned for it.

How to create a content engine that books you 100+ sales calls every month- free mini course 👇🏻

1. Ask ChatGPT to create a content questionnaire:

feed it your sales calls from notetaker, beliefs, product details, ICP pain points

2. Use a notetaker to record everything you say:

raw inputs > perfect drafts

3. Feed that data into a Custom GPT (or Claude) that learns your tone, POV, offers

4. Copy-paste a content calendar from Airtable (just Google: content calendar site:airtable. com)

5. Get 3 people from your team to post content:

Offer small incentives for reach or booked calls

6. Use ChatGPT agents + Perplexity AI for weekly content research (Reddit, Inc., G2, forums, trends)

7. Follow this system for 3 weeks straight:

→ 2 posts/day = 1 lead magnet + 1 workflow/tutorial

8. Build an engagement channel (Telegram, Slack, LinkedIn DM group) with 10–15 target audience accounts who comment first

9. After 1 month, create an AI agent trained on your viral posts, comments, saved replies, and hook styles

10. Repeat weekly → track → scale what converts

⸻

Content will sometimes book you leads faster than ads, cold email, or outbound.

95% of cold email campaigns don’t fail because of copy.

They fail because of lead lists and infrastructure.

Most people don’t know this:

1. Lead Lists

- Catch-alls aren’t equal → validate twice using MillionVerifier + Scrubby | Complete email validation  

- Role-based emails (info@, hr@) silently drag down deliverability

- Enterprise firewalls block cold traffic → cut them early if you see securemail” or “gatedmail” remove them.

2. Infrastructure

- 1 domain = 1 tenant

- 1-2 inboxes max per domain

- Start at 15-20 sends per inbox, ramp slowly

- Randomize send windows daily

- Auto-pause campaigns and put to warmup when bounce >2%

When data + infra are right → 
even average copy lands and books meetings.

When they’re wrong → 
even the best copy ends up in spam.

Cold email doesn’t fail because people can’t write.

It fails because they don’t respect the system behind it.

Last week we audited 28+ b2b saas companies and all of them ignored these 7 signals 

Which can bring them atleast 20-30 leads every week 

1. Ignoring competitor’s reviews

Your competitor’s 1-star reviews are basically your product roadmap. 

Tools like App Radar or even a simple review-tracking bot can flip those complaints into your strongest buying points.

2. Skipping YouTube:

Everyone talks about LinkedIn, but YouTube is still the most underused B2B channel. 

Consistent, educational videos = long-term inbound compounding.

3. Dropping prospects after a demo:

Don’t just wave goodbye when someone says “not now.” 

Add them into a free Slack/Discord channel. Keep nurturing. Most “no’s” eventually flip if they stay in your orbit.

4. Choosing the wrong influencers:

A big LinkedIn following ≠ conversions. 

Check engagement quality and buyer overlap before you spend.

5. Underestimating cold email volume:

Like it or not, volume beats everything. 

Sending 500 emails a month won’t get you anywhere if its not high intent. 

6. Not integrating AI agents:

Agents are trending. 

If you’re not building them into your product workflows and your content, you’re already behind.

7. Ignoring your team’s brands on LinkedIn:

Your team’s LinkedIn activity alone can bring 20–30 extra calls per week if you give them content playbooks and lead magnet posts.

So how did Clay blew up so fast? Was it only influencer marketing? 

I get at least 5 calls every month where founders say the same thing:

“We want a growth plan like Clay.”

Here’s the truth:

Clay didn’t blow up by luck. 

They had a GTM plan so solid that the whole lead gen industry couldn’t ignore them.

Let’s do a breakdown today:

1. They started in a community: 

People started talking about clay inside SYC- led by Jesse the biggest GTM community. All the top GTM/ sales/ founders hang out here. That’s where the first real conversations started.

2. Niche influencer marketing:

They didn’t try to win “everyone.” They went straight to lead gen influencers.

Clay offered them a credit-based plan (something that’s useful) and had them post workflows. Those posts went viral, and soon even micro-creators in the space started talking.

3. Indepth youtube guides:

🦾Eric Nowoslawski built insanely detailed tutorials for niche use cases. Those guides became the playbook everyone used to learn the product.

4. They picked one core audience:

Instead of trying to be for everyone, they went all-in on lead gen agencies. The type of users who could multiply value overnight.

Soon the agencies started introducing clay workflows in their plans and people like Patrick Spychalski even introduced clay agency concept. Which was a massive hit!

5. Clay owned LinkedIn’s feed:

It felt like every scroll had someone talking about Clay. That wasn’t random. It was a coordinated content push.

6. They used partnerships:

Being a third-party platform, they partnered with Smartlead , Instantly.ai, and more. 

Webinars, co-marketing, integrations all pointing back to Clay.

7. They sent millions of emails every month

While you think:

“Oh 20K+ cold emails are too much 😰”

Clay sent millions of cold + warm emails every month. Every influencer campaign was retargeted through inboxes.

And enterprises were targeted through cold emails!

——

But here’s the one thing most people miss:

Clay’s GTM only worked because the product solved a real painful problem that most of the tools in this space like apollo, zoominfo, salesforce didn’t. 

They focused on signals and enrichments in a way that actually got people results from their campaign… with unique workflows! 

We have been copy pasting clay’s GTM funnels for YC-backed companies and large to medium scale b2b saas companies in this space and they work.

One problem with one solution.

Launch your campaign with Smartlead, test variations.

And let the system work for you.

Measure opens, replies, and conversions.

[POST] 2025-10-08 | activity 7381741402856013824 | likes 95 | comments 29 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7381741402856013824

LinkedIn’s AI is deleting your cold DMs!

You may think your offer is bad

Or lead quality is low but…

Something major is happening right now 

We learned it the hard way.

We send high volume of inmails every months.

And here’s what I have learned: 

1. LinkedIn tracks your behavioural fingerprint: 

- It doesn’t just read your message.

- It tracks how you act on the platform.

What triggered it? 

- Logging into 3+ accounts from same IP

- Copy-pasting messages

- Sudden spike in outbound

- Sending while not engaging with feed

What fixes it?

- Dedicated proxies for each profile

- Separate browser sessions (Gologin/ Ghost Browser)

- Warm up with likes, comments, voice notes

2. It flags structural patterns even without automation

What LinkedIn flags: 

Same format.
Same tone.
Same CTA.

What fixes it?

- Use spintax on LinkedIn DMs or paraphrase each DM

- Used post history, mutuals, and company context

- No links, no Calendly, no “quick question”

- Deliberate typos and lowercase text

- Voice notes and custom Looms for certain leads

3. LinkedIn looks at reply patterns not just volume

If too many people ignore you 
→ you get deprioritized.

What fixes it:

- Send to people active in last 7–14 days

- Prioritise 1st-degree warmup before outreach

- Follow 3day nudge with soft reframe

- Re-engaged past convos to build trust on account

We also built an internal dashboard to monitor:

- Connect → accept %

- Message → reply %

- Profile views

- Weekly profile “temperature”

4. LinkedIn treats each profile like a person or a bot

And it knows when you’re trying to game the system.

- One profile per clean mobile proxy

- Randomised actions across dayparts

- Manual login + OTP sync

- Natural click paths between each message

- Every profile now behaves like a unique human.

Because LinkedIn tracks it that way.

Most teams optimise message copy.

Smart teams optimise infrastructure + behaviour.

LinkedIn doesn’t care how good your offer is.

It cares whether you look like a human or a spammer! 

Hope this helps! 

Share it with your team.

[POST] 2025-10-09 | activity 7382040844238761984 | likes 32 | comments 7 | shares 1
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7382040844238761984

How to get everyone talking about your SaaS- A Masterclass 👇🏻

We have done this for multiple med-large saas companies. 

Here are a few that performed the best: 

1. Turn Influencers Into Co-Creators,

Start with mini podcast with influencers and mention them:

Most time they will repost with their audience

“We’re hosting a 45-minute session breaking down how [your product] solves [big painful problem]. 

Want to join and share your angle?”

They get content for their audience.

You get distribution without paying.

2. Start a Reddit, Inc. Thread That Feels Real

Reddit is where virality starts quietly.

We even train our ai agents to farm content from reddit.

Start a thread like:

“We built this after sending 100K cold emails manually here’s what we learned.”

Then:

Show behind-the-scenes screenshots

Share one painful lesson

Casually mention your product at the end

Comment from your team’s accounts (not bots) to push it up.

If the tone feels human, founders will repost it to X, Discord, and Slack groups.

3. Turn Your Beta Users Into Micro-Influencers

Your early users are your best PR team.

Give them small wins and public permission to brag.

Examples:

“We just gave 20 beta users early access. If you’re one, tell us what broke.”

“Whoever finds the funniest bug gets a free lifetime plan.”

People love posting when it’s playful.

4. Make Every Launch Feel Like a Community Moment

Invite creators before launch.

Give them inside jokes, sneak peeks, and early screenshots.

Then drop posts like:

“Shoutout to the 17 people who saw this product before it launched. You know who you are 👀”

That one line makes everyone curious and makes influencers feel part of the movement.

5. Meme Your Way Into Their Feeds

Influencers repost funny truths faster than promo videos.
Use Yes/No memes, Joker format, and Chad vs Virgin templates like:

“For Founder 2, I spent 3 months building a product instead of talking about it”

You can find templates on supermeme

6. Do a ‘Soft Drop’ → Not a Launch

Launches die fast. Conversations live longer.

Start posting 2–3 cryptic teasers before going public:

“Something’s coming that will kill manual outbound forever.”

“This tool almost broke Gmail. We’re fixing that next week.”

By the time you post the official link, the community’s already waiting.

7. Reward Posts, Not Payments

Instead of paying influencers, build a post-reward system.

Example:

“Anyone who posts their honest take on [product] gets 3 free months.”

You’ll get dozens of organic, authentic posts from real users — not forced ads.

8. Build Post-Launch Velocity

The day after your launch:

Drop behind-the-scenes content (“Here’s what actually happened”)

Share early metrics

Tag your webinar guests and Reddit contributors

Keep the narrative alive.
People don’t share your launch.
They share your momentum.

--- 

If you need help with more such ideas DM us :)

🚨Breaking: Google just banned millions of mailboxes used for cold emailing in 24 hours.

Here’s what happened ⬇️

1. Old free GSuite accounts were removed

2. EDU panels with 10,000+ free inboxes were fixed

3. Nonprofit accounts were gone

4. $1 “pre-warmed” reseller panels was banned

If your system was built on cheap inboxes, it’s gone.

Why did Google do this?

Because these setups were never meant for outbound sales. 

When resellers are selling you an inbox for $1, it’s not magic it’s a loophole.

And Google finally shut the door.

And it will even shut more door in the next coming days. 

What still works (and is safe):

✅ Official Google Workspace 
✅ Microsoft 365 inboxes
✅ Private SMTP setups
✅ Diversification across multiple providers

What you should do now?

1. Check your licenses
2. Pause bulk sending for now
3. Diversify infra dont rely on one provider

And make sure that you 👇🏻

1. Focus on clean infra
2. Validated data Scrubby | Complete email validation is the best out there 
3. Relevant copy 

Google didn’t kill cold email.

It killed the shortcuts to avoid spam!

[POST] 2025-11-11 | activity 7393975471283040256 | likes 62 | comments 39 | shares 1
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7393975471283040256

LinkedIn’s AI is deleting your cold DMs!

You may think your offer is bad

Or lead quality is low but…

Something major is happening right now 

We learned it the hard way.

We send high volume of inmails every months.

And here’s what I have learned: 

1. LinkedIn tracks your behavioural fingerprint: 

- It doesn’t just read your message.

- It tracks how you act on the platform.

What triggered it? 

- Logging into 3+ accounts from same IP

- Copy-pasting messages

- Sudden spike in outbound

- Sending while not engaging with feed

What fixes it?

- Dedicated proxies for each profile

- Separate browser sessions (Gologin/ Ghost Browser)

- Warm up with likes, comments, voice notes

2. It flags structural patterns even without automation

What LinkedIn flags: 

Same format.
Same tone.
Same CTA.

What fixes it?

- Use spintax on LinkedIn DMs or paraphrase each DM

- Used post history, mutuals, and company context

- No links, no Calendly, no “quick question”

- Deliberate typos and lowercase text

- Voice notes and custom Looms for certain leads

3. LinkedIn looks at reply patterns not just volume

If too many people ignore you 
→ you get deprioritized.

What fixes it:

- Send to people active in last 7–14 days

- Prioritise 1st-degree warmup before outreach

- Follow 3day nudge with soft reframe

- Re-engaged past convos to build trust on account

We also built an internal dashboard to monitor:

- Connect → accept %

- Message → reply %

- Profile views

- Weekly profile “temperature”

4. LinkedIn treats each profile like a person or a bot

And it knows when you’re trying to game the system.

- One profile per clean mobile proxy

- Randomised actions across dayparts

- Manual login + OTP sync

- Natural click paths between each message

- Every profile now behaves like a unique human.

Because LinkedIn tracks it that way.

Most teams optimise message copy.

Smart teams optimise infrastructure + behaviour.

LinkedIn doesn’t care how good your offer is.

It cares whether you look like a human or a spammer! 

Hope this helps! 

Share it with your team.

They see our message while they’re still in their inbox.

How to find who is clicking your ads or visiting your website? 👀

Most teams don’t actually know who’s visiting their website or who’s clicking their ads.

You see traffic, but you don’t see people.
And that’s where the confusion starts.

➡️ GA4 shows you numbers
➡️ Reverse IP shows you companies
➡️ Forms barely catch anyone

So you end up guessing.

The real goal is simple:

❌ Move from “someone from Adobe viewed pricing”

to

✅ “Sarah Davis from Adobe viewed pricing twice this week.”

That level of detail actually means something.

Here’s how you get there:

➡️ Get clear on your ICP:

Roles, industries, seniority, company size, geography.

Also mark your intent pages: 

pricing, demo, case studies.

➡️ Set up the basics:

GA4, LinkedIn tag, Meta pixel, UTMs.

This only shows where traffic came from.

➡️ Add the Vector 👻 pixel:

This is where everything flips.

Vector doesn’t show “a HubSpot visitor.”

It shows the actual person who landed on your site.

➡️ Clean your feed:

Filter by Director+, the right departments, ICP companies.

Your view becomes clean instantly.

➡️ Turn on Ad Reveal:

Now you see the names of people who clicked your ads

And visited your site..

even if they never filled anything.

➡️ Sync these audiences into LinkedIn, Meta, Google:

Retargeting becomes “people who can actually buy”

not “everyone who visited.”

➡️ When a qualified visitor hits pricing:

Vector sends a Slack ping

updates your CRM

and your team follows up while intent is still warm.

➡️ Stop tracking CTR or sessions:

Start tracking actual revenue signals:
cost per ICP click

You can use Claude + Airtable to generate leads for you.

And only the top 1% actually know how to pull it off.

Here’s how the full system works 👇

You need:

🧠 Claude (Pro)

📋 Airtable

🔁 n8n

📨 Gmail


🔹 Step 1: Claude = The Research Brain:

Inside Claude Desktop, you create a **Project** that does this:

“Find small businesses in [City] that match [my criteria], score them, and extract:

- Business name
- Website
- Pain point
- Modernisation opportunity
- AI solution
- Email (if found)
- Lead score (1–10)”

Claude:

- Uses real-time search
- Reads their websites
- Analyses operations
- Qualifies them
- And preps all data — automatically

🔹 Step 2: Claude sends data to Airtable via MCP + n8n

Here’s where it gets slick:

- Claude uses an MCP trigger
- That trigger sends the lead data to n8n*
- n8n parses the lead
- Then inserts a new row in Airtable with:

    - Business Name
    - Website
    - Score
    - Problem + Opportunity
    - Solution idea

Every time you run the Claude prompt, your Airtable lead list grows scored and cleaned.

🔹 Step 3 High-score leads trigger auto-written emails

A second n8n workflow checks Airtable every 1 min:

If Score ≥ 7:

✅ AI writes a custom pitch

✅ Saves it in **your Gmail Drafts**

✅ Pushes the email subject + body back into Airtable

Low-score leads?

Ignored.

High-score leads?

Auto-drafted emails, ready to hit send.

🔹 Real-world example

You type:

“Find Software businesses in Salt Lake City.”

Claude:

- Finds 10
- Scores 4 as qualified
- Sends them to Airtable
- Those 4 get pitch emails written + drafted in Gmail

You:

- Open Gmail
- Review
- Hit send

That’s it.

This system is:

- Specific
- Score-based
- Structured

We analysed 200,000+ cold emails. What we learned was uncomfortable.

73% of cold emails had few patterns that explains the neuroscience behind it.

These are the patterns we noticed in better reply rate/campaign performance: 

- Prospect’s brain prioritises relevance first
- Existing pain triggers better attention
- Third party mentions get better replies
- Low effort emails gets more response
- Urgency creates decisions
- Soft signals get you better response 
- Timing beats creativity
- Volume beats everything else
- Shorter the offer better the response

Cold outreach works when you stop trying to impress.

And start fitting into the your prospect’s existing decision process.

[POST] 2026-01-05 | activity 7413941571341099008 | likes 74 | comments 20 | shares 0
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7413941571341099008

Cold outreach don’t get replies because they’re persuasive.

They get replies

because they pass three mental filters.

Most emails fail at filter one.

1. Cognitive Ease

The brain avoids effort by default.

If your name, brand, or idea feels unfamiliar,
the brain categorises it as work.

Work gets skipped.

- Familiar patterns feel easy.
- Easy feels safe.
- Safe gets opened.

2. Mere Exposure Effect

People trust what they’ve seen repeatedly
even if they don’t remember where.

- A post.
- A comment.
- A like.
- A shared idea.

By the time your email lands,
you’re not new.

You’re already processed.

That lowers resistance before your copy starts.

3. Threat Reduction

Unknown outreach triggers one question:

“Why is this person in my inbox?”

Not
“Is this valuable?”
Not
“Is this smart?”

Familiarity answers the threat question instantly.

No threat → no defence → reply.

This is why cold outreach fails
when you treat the message as the start.

The message is the last step.

Visibility does the persuasion.

Repetition does the trust-building.

The email just collects the reply.

#coldemailpsychology #acquisitionx

[POST] 2026-01-06 | activity 7414286662861508608 | likes 91 | comments 35 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7414286662861508608

Most companies in 2026 will die because they are not building distribution funnels.

If you think only cold outreach or posting content is enough then think again. 

Here’s how to actually build a distribution engine for your company in 2026: 

1. Start with one behaviour, not a platform:

Don’t ask “LinkedIn or YouTube?”

Ask: What does my buyer do right before they buy?

Examples:
	•	searches comparisons
	•	asks peers
	•	reads case studies
	•	checks DMs
	•	watches walkthroughs

You build around that behaviour. The platform comes later.

2. Build one asset that lives longer than a post

Examples:
	•	a comparison page
	•	a recurring series
	•	a free tool
	•	a living doc
	•	a resource people return to

If it can’t be revisited, it can’t distribute.

3. Create forced return paths:

Distribution fails when people have no reason to come back.

You design reasons:

	•	updates
	•	follow-ups
	•	sequels
	•	continuations
	•	progressions

If nothing pulls them back, awareness dies.

4. Layer visibility, not channels:

You don’t “expand to more platforms”.
You let the same thing appear in multiple places.

One core asset → sliced → echoed → referenced.

The system distributes. You don’t.

5. Convert only after familiarity exists:

You wait until:

	•	they’ve seen you before
	•	they recognise your point of view
	•	they already trust the direction

Then conversion feels obvious, not pushy.

How you know this is working?

	•	People reference things you made
	•	Conversations start mid-context
	•	You explain less
	•	Decisions happen faster


#distributionfunnelsfor2026

(Reshared from: Peculiar Palak Jain)

I stopped replying to clients immediately.

Earlier, I thought fast replies = professionalism.

But fast replies actually gave me:
• half-written answers
• unclear sentences
• wrong, too casual, too rushed tone
• follow-up messages to fix what I missed

Not a great look.

Then I heard Suprava Sabat say something that hit hard:
When you reply too fast, you reply without thinking.

So I checked my old replies.
She was right.

Now this is my rule:

When a message or email comes in
→ I don’t reply instantly
→ I sit with it for 5 minutes
→ I think: What do I really want to say?
→ I draft it
→ I refine it (yes, with GPT or any tool)

Because words matter.
Tone matters.
Clarity matters.

One rushed reply can:
• confuse a client
• look careless
• slowly harm your reputation

And reputation is a long-term game.

So here’s my advice for this year:

Don’t reply quickly. Reply thoughtfully.
Unless it’s urgent, give your words the respect they deserve.

Your future self (and your clients) will thank you.

[POST] 2026-01-12 | activity 7416471503682195456 | likes 78 | comments 29 | shares 2
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7416471503682195456

Most cold outreach fails for one stupid reason.

You are asking for a meeting before their brain has said yes to anything.

Neuroscience shows people say yes faster after they have already said yes once.

That is called the Commitment and Consistency Principle.

When you ask:

“Got 15 minutes for a call?”

That sentence activates the same threat response as a credit card checkout page.

Here is what actually books meetings:

1. You must get a “yes” before you ever ask for time:

The human brain is wired to stay consistent with past behaviour.

Once someone agrees to anything, even something tiny, their probability of agreeing again jumps massively.

Your first message should never be a meeting request. 

It should be something frictionless like access, curiosity, or validation.

2. The second “yes” is where most money is made:

The second micro-agreement is what creates internal pressure.

Now they have taken two actions in the same direction.

Backing out feels psychologically wrong, even if it is free.

3. The call is not a pitch:

By the time you ask for 10 minutes, their brain already thinks they are “in this.”

You are not convincing them.

You are simply giving them a way to stay consistent with what they already did.

How to find the people who actually reply to cold emails? 

Every founder and sales rep fights for the same inbox.

The Head of departments and VP inbox.

Those get 100+ emails a day.

Most never get opened.

But one level below them sits someone nobody targets.

The people who reply are the ones who get hit first when things break.

Every problem has a small radius.

When pipeline drops, who hears it first?

When reps underperform, who gets the Slack messages first?

Ask yourself!

That’s who you email.

Not the person with the biggest title.

The person with the earliest pain.

Examples people miss:

- The SDR manager when meetings dry up
- The CRM admin when routing breaks

[POST] 2026-02-06 | activity 7425575257463214080 | likes 80 | comments 26 | shares 8
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7425575257463214080

How the top 1% make cold email work in 2026

Here’s what has been working:

1. Account thinking > lead thinking

Most people ask:
Who can I email?

The best teams ask:
Who actually influences this buying decision?

before sending anything, they check for intent:
-website visits
-content engagement
-buying signals

Tool: Trigify.io 

It helps you focus on accounts that already show interest instead of guessing.

2. Small targeted lists > big broad lists
This is where most outbound fails.

Sending to 50k–100k leads feels productive.
It usually isn’t.

Top teams work with micro-lists:
-500–1,000 hyper-targeted prospects
- very clear ICP
- a clear intent to reach out now

Instead of dumping thousands of unverified emails, they:
-find emails for the exact roles they want
-verify emails before sending anything
-handle catchalls properly instead of guessing
-keep bounce rates low and lists clean

Tool: Icypeas.

3. Real signals > generic openers

Instead of writing:
“Hey John, hope all is well at Company”

High-performing teams reference something real:
-a recent job posting
-a funding announcement
-a new case study
-Technologies used

When the first line feels relevant, people keep reading.

Signals tell you what to talk about, not just who to email.

4. Simple sequences > one perfect email

Most replies don’t come from the first email.

they come from strong sequences, usually look like this:

Email 1
A simple question to start the conversation.

without any pitch.

Email 2 (3–5 days later)
-More personalized.
-Introduce your value offer.
-Offer to share something useful.

Email 3 (3–5 days later)

Same message, different angle.
Short and direct.

Email 4 (3–5 days later)
Clean breakup email.

Tool: Smartlead
 
5. Value first > meeting requests
Top reps don’t push calls immediately.

Instead of:
“Can we hop on a call?”

They say:
“Want me to send a quick breakdown?”
“Happy to share an example.”
“Should I send something that might help?”

That’s it. 

If this helped, I’ll share more breakdowns like this.

What’s harder for you right now:
finding the right audience, getting replies, or starting conversations?

Just watched Suprava Sabat's take on lead generation for 2026 on Smartlead's YouTube, and it reinforced one big truth:

Spray-and-pray outbound is dead!
Like, actually dead. 

The people still blasting 1,000 cold emails a day?
They're basically shouting into a void, wearing a suit, pretending it's a strategy.

Here's what's actually working in 2026:

Timing > Volume
Stop chasing everyone. Start chasing people who are already snooping around. Website visits, intent signals, and engagement data are your golden breadcrumbs. Follow them.

LinkedIn is NOT a pitch machine
Post. Be consistent. Be human. So when you finally slide into someone's DMs, they don't immediately think "ugh, another one." They think, "Oh, I know this person!"

Cold email isn't dead; it's just on life support if you do it wrong!
One generic email = trash folder.
Sequenced + personalised + followed up = actual replies.

Multichannel or bust
LinkedIn + Email + Retargeting + Smart follow-ups.
One touchpoint is a whisper. Multiple thoughtful ones are a conversation.

AI = your intern, not your strategist
Let it handle signals, sequences, and data enrichment.
Let "you" handle the thinking. (Yes, you still have to do this part)

The bottom line?
The 2026 pipeline isn't about who shouts loudest.
It's about who shows up smarter!

So the real question is, are you still grinding volume like it's 2019... or have you actually built a system?

• LinkedIn posts
 • Lead magnets
 • Value threads
 • Case studies

→ Smartlead

• post engaged with
 • problem category
 • ICP segment

Warm outreach converts significantly higher than cold.

Tools:

→ Livestorm  
→ Luma

Engaged users invited
 ↓
 Attendees segmented by participation
 ↓
 Hot leads routed to sales
 ↓
 Cold attendees recycled into nurture

 → ReactIn (extract engagers)
 → Clay (enrichment + segmentation)

Competitor post detected
 ↓
 ReactIn captures engagers
 ↓
 Trigify flags buying signals
 ↓
 Clay enriches + scores
 ↓
 Warm outreach triggered

Attention is intercepted mid-journey.

Purpose: Run cold only when signals exist.

→ Amplemarket HQ   
 → GojiberryAI  
 
Sequencing:

 → Smartlead SmartAgents

SmartAgents handle:

• reply classification
• automated follow-ups

• uninterested prospect pausing

Cold becomes systematic.

→ Smartlead

Workflow:

Reply detected
 ↓
 Slack alert
 ↓
 AI drafts response
 ↓
 Human confirms

 Call placed if needed

Sub-3 minute response target.

[RESHARE] 2026-03-12 | activity 7437800819774816256 | likes 27 | comments 8 | shares 1
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7437800819774816256
(Reshared from: Amna Ismail)

IMPERFECTION IS PERFECTION

Not long ago, if someone wrote an email in all small letters, people would think:
“Does this person even know how to write a proper email?”

Perfect grammar, polished vocabulary, and structured formatting used to signal professionalism.

But things have changed.

Recently I watched a webinar where a speaker Suprava Sabat suggested something surprising:

When writing cold emails, try using simple sentences and even small letters.

Why?

Because today, when people see a perfectly written message with flawless grammar and structured headings, their first thought is often:
“Was this written by ChatGPT or a bot?”

Ironically, what once looked unprofessional now signals authenticity.

A slightly imperfect message…
A casual tone…
Even small letters…

These things now make people feel there is a real human behind the message.

The digital world has flipped the script. Maybe the new rule is simple:
Imperfection is the new perfection.

How to get anyone’s reply with the “mirror technique” 👇🏻

90% cold emails looks like this: 

“We offer xyz”
“We can add xyz in abc” 
“Our solution” 

And this is the reason “cold email is dead” 

This is how you can make it work: 

People reply when they see themselves in your email

This is how it can look like:

Example:

Instead of saying:
“We help SaaS companies improve outbound.”

Say:

“Most SaaS founders I speak with are sending thousands of cold emails…
but 40–50% of those emails never even reach the inbox.”

Suddenly the reader thinks:

“Wait… that might be happening to us.”

That moment is the mirror.

They see their own problem in your email.

And when someone sees their own situation described better than they can describe it…

They reply.

Breaking 🚨- email is about to become… AI talking to AI.

Here’s a prediction most people in outbound aren’t thinking about yet:

Very soon, agents will send cold emails to other agents.

Let me explain.

Right now, SDRs run outbound campaigns.
They write emails, build lists, send sequences.

But with AI agents getting better every month, entire outbound systems will soon run automatically.

But here’s the interesting part.

The person receiving the email won’t be the first one reading it anymore.

Their AI agent will.

Instead of decision-makers digging through 100+ cold emails every day, an agent will:

• scan every incoming email
• understand the company’s needs
• filter the irrelevant messages
• surface only the few that actually matter

So the future looks like this:

Agent → sends outbound email
Agent → receives and evaluates it
Human → only sees the relevant opportunities

In other words:

AI will fight the spam problem… by becoming the gatekeeper.

This is already starting.

YC just funded AgentMail (YC S25) to build infrastructure specifically for AI-to-AI email communication.

We’re moving toward a world where:

Cold email isn’t written for humans first.
It’s written for agents.

And the companies that learn how to convince the agent first will win outbound.

(Reshared from: Riddhiman Bania)

Why does most LinkedIn posts sound absolutely same?

I was just reading this LinkedIn post where a guy said that he doesn't find the ideas for his content, doesn't research by his own, and neither write his own content.

He has a "Swarm of AI agents" in Claude that does all this for him.

And then I found my answer.

Every other day, I see posts written by AI talking blatantly about AI agents that can apparently make you a millionaire overnight.

The same hooks.
The same tonality.
The same words and phrases.
The same cringy comments.

A growing number of CEOs around the world are getting delusional about AI without even understanding how the whole AI and automation works.

Seems like people have just stopped thinking for themselves. Getting completely relied on AI (that is trained on a limited training data).

For example:

People are obsessing over Claude skills, which are technically nothing but text prompts and tool calling scripts.

But even if you want to make a Claude skill that can write quality LinkedIn posts, you need to know how to write good content yourself, otherwise how are you going to explain it to Claude?

AI models aren't bad, and AI isn't my problem.

I use it daily for:

- DevOps & automation
- Building small custom scrapers
- Open source deployment
- Content research
- many more use cases

But for each of them to work properly I have to research and build the logic manually that it will work on.

I hate when people just post literally anything to get views.

Yes, AI for most people has become another channel of marketing.

Here's a tip:

Read more. Think better.

If you warm call people visiting your website within 24 hrs it can be a game changer.

- identifies the person and company behind every website visit in real time. 

2. Filter by ICP in Clay or AI Ark:

It enriches the profile:

- job title
- company size
- industry
- tech stack 

and filters out anyone who doesn't match your ICP. 

You only move forward with the ones that actually matter.

3. Find the verified mobile with BetterContact (absolutely best in the game)

BetterContact runs a waterfall across every major data source to find the verified mobile number. 

If it exists, BetterContact finds it.

4. Call within 24 hours Context is everything. 

Say like: "Hey [Name] — I noticed someone from your team was on our site looking at [page]. Wanted to reach out personally before the week ended. And offer you a little gift that can might help” 

You can add some funny one liners in the beginning

You reach out to only folks that may have a need and already know about you guys! 

The conversation is already warm before you dial.

Physical mail clubs might kill newsletters…

Because..

Every day it’s:

* another AI-written insight
* another growth hack thread turned into a newsletter

B2C brands are already moving physical mail clubs 

Here’s why it works:

We’ve spent years making communication faster and more scalable.

Now people are craving the opposite:

something slower, more intentional, and more human.

Especially in B2B.

Most founders and executives already get flooded with:

* emails
* LinkedIn messages
* Slack notifications
* automated follow-ups

So when something physical shows up on their desk, it naturally stands out.

This is why I think more and more B2B brands should adopt to physical mail clubs! 

I don’t think newsletters will disappear completely.

But I do think the bar is changing.

[RESHARE] 2026-06-12 | activity 7471104667939303425 | likes 81 | comments 166 | shares 7

(Reshared from: Priyanka S)

Someone sent me this email today:
“Loved what you’re building with your ecommerce growth agency.”

Wait whatttt?
I don’t run an ecommerce growth agency.

So now I have 2 questions:
- What list did they buy?
- Who let them press send?

This is what bad enrichment does.

Your copy can be short, with a clear offer 

But if your data is wrong, 

your outreach becomes a screenshot of someone’s post 😂 

Here’s how I’d do outreach:

1- Start with the account
Not the email.

Check the company first.

-Who do they sell to?
-What do they sell?
-Do they actually fit your offer?

If the account is wrong, the contact is useless.

2- Pick the person based on the problem
-Founder for early-stage GTM.
-Head of Sales for pipeline.
-RevOps for data and systems.

Don’t pitch lead gen to the finance intern.

3- Enrich after the fit is clear

This is how I found QuickEnrich.

I wasn’t looking for another data tool.

I was tired of cutting good leads because credits were expensive.

QuickEnrich gives:
>Verified emails
>Verification date
>Mobile numbers
>LinkedIn URLs
>Company data with job titles.

So, now I can enrich the full list first.

Then decide based on fit.

Not based on which lead felt cheap enough to remove

4- Please stop pitching in the first message.
- Engage with their content on LinkedIn.
- Stand out in the comment section.
- In the meantime, send one simple email around their pain point.
- Provide value first.
Start the conversation naturally.

Pitch later only if relevant.

That’s how outbound works.

[RESHARE] 2026-06-15 | activity 7472277526367649792 | likes 125 | comments 198 | shares 12
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7472277526367649792
(Reshared from: Yannic Schwock)

We tracked 16 months of warmup traffic data across 6M+ domains

Here’s what we found👇🏼 (and why it affects your deliverability)

𝗧𝗵𝗲𝗿𝗲'𝘀 𝗮 𝗯𝗶𝗴 𝘀𝗵𝗶𝗳𝘁 𝗶𝗻 𝗪𝗮𝗿𝗺𝘂𝗽 𝗣𝗼𝗼𝗹 𝗖𝗼𝗺𝗽𝗼𝘀𝗶𝘁𝗶𝗼𝗻
(𝘥𝘦𝘳𝘪𝘷𝘦𝘥 𝘦𝘴𝘵𝘪𝘮𝘢𝘵𝘦 𝘧𝘳𝘰𝘮 𝘰𝘶𝘳 𝘪𝘯𝘵𝘦𝘳𝘯𝘢𝘭 𝘸𝘢𝘳𝘮𝘶𝘱 𝘵𝘳𝘢𝘧𝘧𝘪𝘤 𝘥𝘢𝘵𝘢 𝘵𝘳𝘢𝘤𝘬𝘦𝘥 𝘢𝘤𝘳𝘰𝘴𝘴 𝘢𝘭𝘭 𝘮𝘢𝘪𝘯 𝘴𝘦𝘲𝘶𝘦𝘯𝘤𝘦𝘳𝘴 𝘤𝘰𝘮𝘣𝘪𝘯𝘦𝘥)

𝟮𝟬𝟮𝟱: 34% Google / 52% Microsoft / 14% SMTP & Other
𝟮𝟬𝟮𝟲: 18% Google / 71% Microsoft / 11% SMTP & Other

---

𝗪𝗵𝘆 𝘁𝗵𝗶𝘀 𝗵𝗮𝗽𝗽𝗲𝗻𝘀:
→ Market Adoption of Azure and Outlook Infrastructure.
→ More Microsoft IPs entering the pools, Google Share diluting.
→ Sequencers struggle to naturally balance their warmup pools,
→ They’d need to recruit or buy Google Seed mailboxes, which is expensive 

---

Here’s why this shift becomes concerning for deliverability industry-wide ↓

Most cold email lists are Google-heavy. If you’re warming on a Microsoft heavy warmup pool with little to no Google mailbox exchanges:

→ You’re building little, to no reputation to Google.
→ As a result, your Deliverability to Google suffers.

---

𝗨𝗻𝘁𝗶𝗹 𝗻𝗼𝘄, 𝘄𝗲’𝘃𝗲 𝗮𝗹𝗹 𝗯𝗲𝗲𝗻 𝗯𝗹𝗶𝗻𝗱 𝘁𝗼 𝘁𝗵𝗶𝘀:

The sequencer dashboard shows a warmup score, it says 100%

This score measures if warmup emails inboxed or not.

What it doesn’t show is the composition of the pool.

It’s hidden data that users don’t have access to.

Hidden data on a variable that can make or break your deliverability.

---

𝗚𝗼𝗼𝗱 𝗻𝗲𝘄𝘀 𝗶𝘀:
the composition data is measurable.

You simply need to track which mailboxes are on the other end of your warmup traffic.

Every warmup email (in or out) is a data point. Log this at scale and the average pool composition can be computed from your infrastructure's sample inside the pool.

We have been doing exactly that.

sending.ac users have had access to this data for a while now and they can see what their infrastructure is actually warming against.

---

We realized the problem does not stop at our customer base.

Everyone should have visibility into the composition of their warmup pool.


𝗦𝗼 𝘁𝗼𝗱𝗮𝘆 𝘄𝗲’𝗿𝗲 𝗺𝗮𝗸𝗶𝗻𝗴 𝗼𝘂𝗿 𝗱𝗮𝘁𝗮 𝗽𝘂𝗯𝗹𝗶𝗰:

We built a live index that shows you the average warmup pool composition of every major sequencer’s warmup pool we are tracking through our infrastructure.

→ Google percentage. Microsoft percentage. SMTP percentage.

One glance at the dashboard and you’ll see if your pool has a sufficient Google share.


𝗔𝗻𝗱 𝗺𝗼𝘀𝘁 𝗶𝗺𝗽𝗼𝗿𝘁𝗮𝗻𝘁𝗹𝘆

We are working with the big sequencers to make sure the data is as accurate as it can be, given the obvious restrictions (we only track samples → can’t survey 100% of their user base)

If you want an accurate picture of your own data, we have a way to track it for u and provide a solution path within the sequencer you are already using.

---

Want to get access?

1️⃣ Connect with me so I can DM
2️⃣ Comment “warmup report”

I’ll send you access within a few hours.

[RESHARE] 2026-06-16 | activity 7472594971423080449 | likes 74 | comments 93 | shares 8
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7472594971423080449
(Reshared from: Priyanka S)

A 13 year old kid sent me a cold email asking why his emails are landing in spam.
(Screenshot of the email is in the comments)

I had to create this post since the kid asked me to 

Tbh the copy was actually decent.

But Gmail still sent it to spam.

That’s the part most people miss.

Cold email is not just a copy problem anymore.

You can write the cleanest email in the world 
and still land in spam if your setup is broken.

Here’s what I’d check first:

1- Fix deliverability before touching the copy

Before rewriting the subject line, check the basics:
- Mailbox health.
- Sending setup.
- Campaign status.
- Spam triggers.
- Domain issues.

How I do it:
I open SmartAssistant inside Smartlead and ask:

“Why are my emails landing in spam?”

It checks the account, campaign status, sending setup, 
and possible issues instead of giving generic cold email advice.

2- Don’t send too much from one inbox

Sending 20 emails/day per inbox is safe

If you’re sending high volume from one mailbox, 
you’re asking Gmail to flag you.

How I do it:
I ask SmartAssistant:

“Check if my sending limits are safe.”

Then I fix volume before scaling.

3- Make the email fit on one phone screen

If your cold email needs scrolling, it is too long.

People check emails between calls, meetings, and random 30-second gaps.
Make it easy to read.

How I do it:
I ask SmartAssistant:

“Cut this email so it fits on one mobile screen.”

4- Don’t email prospects 5 times in 10 days

Too much email density makes you look desperate.

How I do it:
I ask SmartAssistant:

“Check my follow-up sequence. Is this too aggressive?”

 Then use it to space out follow-ups across email, LinkedIn, DMs, and calls.

5- Stop asking for a meeting too early

“Can we jump on a 30-minute call?” is too much friction.

Ask a small yes/no question instead.

How I do it:

I ask SmartAssistant:

“Rewrite this CTA into a low-friction yes/no question.”

That’s it.

Before blaming your copy, check the system.

If you’re facing the same issue SmartAssistant actually helps you fix.

It’s live inside Smartlead all you have to do is just ask.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[POST] 2026-06-17 | activity 7472986827948490752 | likes 199 | comments 434 | shares 9
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7472986827948490752

After sending 10,000+ voice notes on LinkedIn here's everything i learned:

Most LinkedIn voice notes get a 👍 instead of a reply.

Here's why - the problem isn't the format. It's 2 things you're skipping.

✦ No consent before sending. Strangers don't owe you their ears. 
Add a short text note first.
✦ Scripts that sound like every other SDR. 
If they replay it 2-3 times trying to figure out your ask, you've already lost them.

Effort ≠ reply "Nice touch" + a reaction is not a conversion. 
Stop confusing engagement with interest. 

Here are the 3 scripts that convert:
✦ The Permission Primer - One line before the voice note that makes them want to listen.
✦ The 20-Second Rule - If your note runs longer, cut it. 
✦ The Single Ask Close - One question or CTA. Nothing else.

I use SalesRobot.co to send these at scale. 

I've put together how you can start by sending voice notes the right way.

Comment "SEND" and I'll DM it to you.
