[POST] 2026-06-19 | activity 7473640483085361153 | likes 151 | comments 122 | shares 8
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7473640483085361153

This is going to be the most important post in GTM you'll read this week. Listen closely, GTM is changing massively. 👇

I spoke to GTM teams at Canva, Perplexity, Gamma AI and 7 more companies.

And what they mentioned is going to change the future of GTM.

We all know how fast moving this space is…

Here are some things that are going to make a big difference in the coming months:

1. Teams are shifting more offline, so event networking communities are going to see a massive boost.

2. Having an educational content YouTube channel is going to have a massive impact on how we get customers. Most of the attention is shifting back to long-form because of AI slop on text-based platforms like LinkedIn, X and more!

3. Cold emailing is going to see a drastic change. A lot of new AI tools for cold emails will be introduced that are going to change how we send emails.

4. Offline gifts are going to become a way to do outreach. Sending cool gifts as outreach!

5. The creativity and art side is going to grow more, and more campaigns will have a creative shift in them. We see this already happening with Silicon Mania  

6. Signals are going to change, micro-intent is going to take place. 

Example: earlier someone raised funding and you reached out; now it will be someone raised funding, but you reach out based on their behavior, like who they're hiring, what the age gap is, decision maker interests and more 

7. More and more teams are going to hire in-house UGC creators as their content team to produce content for them.

8. Teams are going to shift from a 10+ tool stack to all-in-one tools. We will see a major shift here.

9. AI will read your emails before passing them to a human, so whatever email you write needs to pass the AI filters.

10. Offline newsletters are going to take place. You'll notice brands opening offline newsletters (it has already started in the B2C space) curated letters in mailboxes for every new launch, instead of a newsletter in the inbox.

11. We will see a massive boost in community-building GTM activities, especially things like run clubs, tech cafés, and offline meetups dedicated to each brand.

As someone who does GTM for a living, I'm excited to see these changes take place.

If you're someone who can help me implement the above GTM methods for our clients, feel free to reach out to me :) I'm always looking for you.

I've also asked my team to do more research and package the conversation into a playbook.

If you want the step-by-step process, just comment "GTM" and I'll send it your way.

[POST] 2026-06-22 | activity 7474724550795104256 | likes 146 | comments 110 | shares 10
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7474724550795104256

I found a GitHub repo that turns job posts into cold email campaigns.

Here’s how you can use it:

Most people use job posts only to apply for jobs.

Wrong use case.

Job posts are public buying signals.

Notice the job description instead of only going after job role:

You will notice mini keywords:
“build lead lists”
 “manage outbound campaigns”
 “clean CRM data”

It means they likely have:
- bad lead data
- low meeting volume
- manual prospecting work

That is not just a hiring post.

That is a sales signal.

When a company is hiring for a role, they are basically telling you:
→ what is broken
 → what they need fixed
 → what team is under pressure
 → what budget they are about to spend
 → who likely owns the problem

I found a GitHub repo called b2b-outbound-sniper.

That will find you mini signals 

Once you've identified the target company:

Step 1: Use Prospeo.io's People Search to find the exact person who owns the pain.
 
Step 2: Filter by department, seniority, and job function. 

Step 3: Verified emails and mobile numbers, data refreshed every 7 days, across 300M+ B2B contacts.

Step 4: Then push to Smartlead and run the campaign.

Most outbound teams are still buying lists.

The smart teams are reading public pain and turning it into a pipeline.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I disappeared for 20 days and it grew my business even more…

I traveled three countries in that time met people doing 10x better than me… 

And what I learned in those 20 days you will not find on any books  

Lemme share what I learned and saw:

1. one founder cold emails companies that just LOST funding rounds. not raised, lost. they're desperate to show traction and nobody else is emailing them. his reply rates are 3x industry average.

2. there’s a team in dubai that targets only folks spending crazy on PR like podcast/articles and their conversion rate is 30% more than anyone in their industry 

3. a swiss founder in bali creates campaigns on his competitors' angry customers complaints have the same thing in the email body as well he has g2 alerts on 1-star reviews of rivals.

4. A gtm team was tired because every agency told them their “offer is too saturated” so they went the old way the customers that churned they sent them physical gifts which resulted in 40% meeting rate on enterprise deals.

5. one agency's entire outbound is replying to prospects' posts with 2 likes, not 2000. the founder actually reads your reply when nobody else showed up.

I noticed a pattern behind all 5…

they stopped fighting for attention where everyone is looking.

leads aren't hard to find. 

they're just hiding where your tools don't look.

Take this post as a sign to take that trip you have in your bucket list…

We are sending these handwritten notes as outreach it got us 22+ positive replies👇🏻

That leaves us to go back to old school ways…

This is our usual process:

1. Ocean.io to find company lookalikes:

We pasted our best client URLs into ocean.

It gives us 100 companies with the exact same DNA

Add them to ur LinkedIn 

2. Claude Code to found the hidden trigger

For each company we ask Claude:

We find some psychology concepts that is publicly available posted by the team or founder

Then we map companies having a job opening for more than 3 months 

3. Asked claude to turn into a realistic handwritten letter 

Every prospect got a DM that looked like this : 

A photo of a handwritten note.

With their pain point with our offer!

We do this manually you have to too

[POST] 2026-07-10 | activity 7481312129632030722 | likes 103 | comments 23 | shares 3
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7481312129632030722

Do you know you have 0.3 seconds to capture your prospect’s attention?

And no I’m not saying this 

It’s based on neuroscience. 

Learn psychology before you even touch outreach here are some concepts (tried and tested) 

Use these today and watch the difference: 

1. squint-test your message.

blur your eyes and look at it. 

what’s the shape? 

try to scan the msg before you send is it scanable? 

2. have attachments in your follow-ups

note, meme, report card, receipt, whiteboard photo. 

objects trigger curiosity in the pre-conscious window. 

sentences trigger sorting.

3. break the grammar of outreach. 

every cold message starts with a hey. 

You open mid-thought: “94 days. that’s how long your gtm role has been open.” 

the brain can’t classify a message that starts in the middle it has to read to find out where it is.

4. open with a mistake or a loss. 

“we pitched a company like yours last month. here’s what I got wrong.” 

the brain cannot tag self-inflicted damage as a sales pattern 

salespeople never lead with losing.

5. reference something only a human would notice. 

pick behaviours that doesn’t look like mass generated or scraped by an ai 

6. remove the ask entirely.

“already found 3 gaps in your outbound. attached what we found no call needed.”

the brain sorts messages into asks vs gifts on sight.

7. give a tiny instruction.

“scroll to slide 3. that one’s about you.”

people always wanna see what’s in it for themselves! 

———

I have more such 100+ concepts you can use today lmk if you need. 

Share this post with ur team!

[POST] 2026-07-16 | activity 7483499536531832834 | likes 113 | comments 28 | shares 5
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7483499536531832834

We reverse-engineered our best clients and turned it into 37+ positive replies👇🏻

Here’s what we did:

Most lead lists are built using basic filters:

- industry
- headcount
- location
- job title

But companies can match all four and still be completely wrong.

So we worked backwards from clients who had already paid us.

Find the common pattern

We checked:

→ what they sell
→ who they sell to
→ their business model
→ why they hired us

Find similar companies

We pasted our best client domains into 
Prospeo’s company lookalike.

It helped us find companies that were similar based on what they actually do, not just industry tags.

Find one clear trigger

For each company, we checked:

→ long-term job openings
→ founder posts
→ new hires
→ company news

We only contacted companies where we found a clear reason to reach out.

Turn it into handwritten outreach 

Claude connected the trigger with our offer and wrote a short note.

Then we turned it into a realistic handwritten letter and sent the image through LinkedIn.

Every note mentioned:

- their company
- their current problem
- our offer

No generic pitch.

This works the best if you have a nieche market to sell to and they are active on LinkedIn. 

Drop ur questions below 👇🏻 

Share this workflow with your team

(If you want us to implement the same workflow to your system feel free to dm)

[POST] 2026-07-22 | activity 7485681790473531392 | likes 94 | comments 129 | shares 10
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7485681790473531392

We turned Product Hunt launches into 787+ verified leads 

Here's the exact workflow.

1) Scrape product hunt with our claude skill 

We have a built a skill on top of n8n that scrapes product hunt every week and maps new launches that fits our TAM

2) Filter for the launches that actually fit

Not every launch is a buyer.

We filter by category, funding stage, and team size.

Upvotes etc

3) Push the list into A-Leads API 🔥

This is the part that changed everything.

Instead of manually looking up each founder, we hit the A-Leads API with the company domain and the role we want.

It returns:

- Work email
- Personal email
- Mobile number
- LinkedIn profile

All verified. 

The best part is they only charge for the data that actually validates. 

Unlike other data providers in the market they charge 70% lower while maintaining the quality as they update their database on weekly basis.

So we save big numbers here without burning an insane amount of credits.


4) Map the campaign to the launch itself

This is where the reply rate comes from.

We don't send "hey saw you launched, congrats."

We map the campaign to what the launch tells us:

Launched a product → they need users → we lead with distribution
Launched with a waitlist → they need conversion → we lead with activation
Launched and got roasted in comments → they need positioning → we lead with the fix

We have a claude skill that writes the email based on this

5) Fire it while the launch is still warm

Same-day send. 

A founder on launch day is checking every notification.

——

The whole thing runs on a schedule now.

——

Connect with me first so the DM actually goes through 🙏

We ran 1,240 LkdIn and 310 cold email sequences through CLAUDE FABLE 5 to find what actually gets a reply.

It wasn't the copy.

The pattern was in how much the sender already knew before writing a single word.

Most outbound still targets on three inputs: 

- job title
- company size
- funding stage

Those tell you who to contact. 

None of them tell you why that person would answer this week.

The messages that got replies were built on a different layer:

→ Who just got hired, and who now controls the budget
→ The one problem that ICP is losing sleep over
→ How severe the pain is, and why
→ The posts, podcasts and newsletters they actually consume

None of that sits in a filter. 

It sits scattered across hiring pages, org changes and comment sections, which is why most teams skip it and go back to volume.

So we handed that part to FABLE 5 + One MCP. 

Skills that surface 50+ of these triggers per prospect, then a system that runs on top:

1. Pulls leads already showing buying signals, instead of a scraped list
2. Drafts the DM from what that person did last week, not from a template
3. Books in under 33 words, because nobody reads a pitch inside a DM
4. Runs the follow ups, so nothing sits on "seen" forever

Same list and same sender. 

Reply rates went from 1% to 2% cold, to 25% to 42% warm.

Last week: 12 demos in 5 days, with Fable 5 and the MCP.

The difference is intent.

You're not interrupting a stranger.

You're joining a conversation that person already started in public.

I found 200 companies identical to our highest-paying client.

Don’t build your lead list like this:

- pick an industry
- pick a headcount range
- pick a location
- export contacts

Looks logical but this converts terribly.

Because filters describe what a company looks like not what a company actually does.

That's why your list is full of "perfect ICP matches" that never buy.

Here's the play we ran instead:

Step 1: Take your highest-paying client's domain.

Step 2: Paste it into prospeo.io Company Lookalike.

Step 3: It reads what the company actually does and returns a list of companies with the same DNA.

Step 4: Pull the decision-makers with verified emails and launch.

We got 200 companies back.

Our old filters had surfaced almost none of them. 

The lesson:

Your best client isn't just a case study, they're a search query.

[POST] 2026-08-31 | activity 7500166483767910402 | likes 135 | comments 40 | shares 2
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7500166483767910402

How to know if you are landing in the spam folder? (Process 👇🏻)

You can’t directly see “spam placement” inside Smartlead, but you can catch the warning signs before the whole campaign tanks.

Here’s how:

1. Go to Smartlead → Insights.

2. Check Reply Rate per Mailbox.

If one mailbox suddenly drops while the others are stable, that’s the first one to investigate.

3. Check Bounce Rate.

Insights flags mailboxes trending toward the danger zone before the fleet average hides it.

4. Check Reply Tracking.

It tells you if IMAP, OAuth, permissions, or the mail service itself broke.

5. Check Blocked Mailboxes.

Smartlead tells you whether the inbox needs a reconnect, cooldown, or replacement.
Then hit Summarise.

It gives you a plain-English breakdown of what’s wrong, what was already fixed, and what still needs your approval.

So instead of waiting 2–3 weeks for campaign-level reply rates to collapse, you can catch the exact mailbox causing the problem much earlier.

Let me know if you need a detailed guide on this?

PS: Martin got not chill

[POST] 2026-09-01 | activity 7500517341257179136 | likes 113 | comments 112 | shares 6
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7500517341257179136

Your competitor’s name + one filter = a pre-qualified lead list.

This is probably one of the easiest ways to stop prospecting completely cold accounts.

Say you sell a Clay alternative.

Most people would search:

Sales teams + Industry + 50–200 employees

I’d rather find companies already mentioning Clay somewhere on their website.

Because those companies already know the category.

Here’s the process:

Step 1: Open Prospeo’s Competitive Intel filter

 Step 2: Enter your competitor
 Example: Clay

Step 3: Filter companies mentioning it on their website

Step 4: Add ICP filters
 B2B + 20–500 employees + US

Step 5: Find the relevant buyer
RevOps, Growth, Sales Ops, Head of Sales, etc.

Step 6: Personalize around the existing competitor relationship

Now your outbound can be:

Already knows problem + already knows category + already knows competitor

That is a much better starting point than explaining from zero why your category even matters.

Lemme know if you need the whole playbook of it

How to turn dead leads into sales calls:

Your old cold email campaigns are probably sitting on hundreds of prospects you already paid to find.

- Go to Instantly.ai → open your old campaign.
- Go to Leads → Filter → Completed No reply.
- Select the prospects that still match your ICP.
- Click Move → New Campaign.

Now give them a completely different reason to reply.

Try:

→ a meme made around their company
→ a handwritten note sent before the email
→ a resource solving one tiny problem for them

I put together something with:

1. 10 pattern interrupts you can send
2. meme + handwritten outreach examples
3. resurrection email templates
4. the exact Instantly setup

[RESHARE] 2026-09-04 | activity 7501522424845447168 | likes 35 | comments 12 | shares 2
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7501522424845447168
(Reshared from: Akanksha Ghosalkar)

Day 4 of 30 Days of finding a job in public.

Until now, I have been applying for roles in all the usual, formal ways:

Through LinkedIn, Naukri.com, Indeed, iimjobs.com, Referral, Company Sites, Cold emails and so on 

Lately, I have been binging Suprava Sabat content a lot and I believe people should follow not just for business outreach ideas, but for job outreach as well.

In one of her content pieces, she spoke about the power of handwritten notes and how they can make outreach feel more personal and memorable.

That stayed with me.

So last week, I decided to do something a little out of the box.

⚪ What if job outreach worked like a PR campaign?

Beauty brands send PR packages filled with goodies to influencers, creators and celebrities. The idea is simple to get the product into the hands of the right people and give it a chance to be seen, experienced, and talked about.

So I thought:

Why can't I create a PR kit for myself as a job seeker?

And that's exactly what I did. 

I created an employee job-seeking PR kit for a brand I genuinely wanted to work with.

This wasn't random outreach.

I sent the Head of HR a handwritten letter, making my case for why I believed I would be a good fit.

Along with it, I sent:

→ A plain round-neck T-shirt made at my brother's garment unit
→ A QR code linking to my profile and my festive storytelling playbook

All of this went to one person.

Unfortunately, my profile didn't make the shortlist. But something unexpected happened.

I received a genuinely thoughtful, handwritten reply from the HR and he mentioned that he really liked the way I had approached the outreach and appreciated the effort behind it.

And honestly?

I won't pretend it didn't affect me. It did 
But then I realised something else.

The effort was good enough to be noticed by the person who mattered. And sometimes, that is a signal too.

The timing may not have been right. The fit may not have been right. But the work was noticed.

It is true when we say "Mann ka ho toh acha, naa ho toh aur bhi acha"

And job search is still in progress!

If this is the kind of innovation, grit, and determination you're looking for or know someone hiring for a brand manager or asst brand manager or entrepreneur in residence role focused on brand building in Mumbai than I would love to be considered. 

And even a conversation or a direction toward the right opportunity would mean a lot.

Resume and Portfolio in the comment section

Instead of starting with “hey name” do this to get their attention through your dm👇🏻

2. If you run reddit agency 

Ask this: are you posting content on Reddit right now? How’s that going??? 

3. If you wanna sell your saas 

Ask this: did you see this? (niche feature) is going super viral did you get a chance to use it? 

- Provoke + peak their curiosity 
- Keep it one liner and simple 
- Remove -- em dashes 

You get them to open the DM + get their reply

Turn your Slack into a GTM AI employee that finds you leads.

My team was still doing GTM work across:

- too many tools
- with research
- follow-ups
- reporting
- campaign tasks handled separately.

Here’s what I changed:

- Added viktor.com to Slack.
- Connected viktor to GTM stack like CRM, email tools, spreadsheets, other integrations.
- Gave Viktor our company context, processes, and past decisions so he works from the same information as my team.
- Delegated actual GTM jobs directly inside Slack. 

And he handed work of:
→ SDRs: account research + outbound work
→ AEs: deal research + follow-ups
→ RevOps: pipeline monitoring + reporting
→ Marketing: campaign operations
→ Founder: numbers + GTM updates

Viktor works across the connected tools and brings the output back into Slack.

For sensitive actions like sending something externally, your team approves it before it goes out.

Hire viktor.com for your team. 
$100 in credits included, no card.

stop asking discovery questions on sales calls if you book them through outbound 

we booked 32 something calls for a SaaS company they closed 0 

(story time) 

our process is pretty simple:

> we run the outbound
> get positive replies
> then give the sales team all the in-depth research we have on each prospect
> we ask them to look at the data and make a roadmap to show their prospects on the call

but instead of using it…

they followed traditional product demos asking a lot of questions and giving no value 

noone seriously care about ur saas features so stop showing features 

show them a picture of how their day to day life would look like using ur product and show them how much research u have done 

same thing with service based business or any kind of business  

u are reaching out to them have a good point on call on why you reached out to them

I found a hack to turn my competitor's customer list into my pipeline. Here's how:

First of all nobody hands you their customer list directly 

But customers always leave footprints. 
> Tech stack mentions. 
> Job posts that ask for experience with the tool. 
> We use [Competitor] for..." on the company's own site.

Instantly.ai Agents turns those footprints into pipeline. 

I described the whole workflow in plain English:

"Find companies showing signs they use [Competitor], check which ones match my ICP, research each company, figure out why they might switch, email the qualified prospects, call the non-responders, and tell me in Slack when somebody is interested."

It builds the agent. Then it runs:

Finds the companies where [Competitor] shows up publicly. Signal-based, so it's noisy. That's fine. Step two fixes it.

Researches each one, reads their site, scores them against my ICP, explains why they fit.

Finds the strongest switching angle for each qualified account and writes the email around it. These are the easiest cold emails you'll send. Budget approved. Problem understood. Wrong tool picked.

No reply? It moves to an AI voice call.

Reply comes in? It reads it, detects buying intent, and pauses and ask me in slack before the next step.

Try it here: https://lnkd.in/ee3iAxFJ
Code SUPRAVA20 gets you 20% off.

I turned Claude into a B2B SALES MEETING MACHINE. 
It now generate 5–15 warm leads a month. 2–3 new customers on autopilot.

I didn't want another collection of AI prompts.

I wanted a MEETING ENGINE. A one that constantly improve with intelligence. 

So I built one around four stages :
→ Define
→ Research
→ Outreach
→ Learn

Claude sits across the entire process.

It can help:
→ Find ICP accounts
→ Check buying signals
→ Qualify prospects
→ Research opportunities
→ Draft outreach
→ Learn from replies

The important part isn't any individual prompt.

It's the sequence. And the BRAIN behind it. 

Each step gives context to the next.

50+ data providers and tools behind one call, always routed to the cheapest source that has the answer.

[RESHARE] 2026-10-17 | activity 7517119489650360321 | likes 31 | comments 21 | shares 1
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7517119489650360321
(Reshared from: Vijay Mythreyan)

How to generate $ 100K- $ 500K, and even more, with this cold email lead generation system.

Buttt.... most people are confused about how many cold emails to send daily. 

Here's the math. 👇

You have 22,500 prospects.
You want to send 90,000 emails.

But how many new prospects should you contact daily?

How many follow-ups will add to your sending volume?

And why does a 24-day campaign take 47 days to finish?

Let's break it down.

1. Warm-up days
Days 1–14: Warm up your inboxes.
Day 15: Start your campaign.

2. Calculate your daily prospects
22,500 prospects ÷ 24 campaign days = 938 new prospects/day.

3. Understand your sending volume
Each prospect receives 4 emails:

→ Day 1: First email
→ Day 3: Follow-up 1
→ Day 10: Follow-up 2
→ Day 24: Follow-up 3

22,500 × 4 = 90,000 total potential emails.

But you're not sending all 90,000 at once.

Your daily volume builds as follow-ups kick in.

→ Campaign Day 1: 938 emails
→ Campaign Day 3: 1,876 emails
→ Campaign Day 10: 2,814 emails
→ Campaign Day 24: 3,752 emails

These figures assume 938 new prospects daily and that all scheduled emails are sent, without replies or opt-outs.

4. When does the campaign finish?

→ Days 1–14: Inbox warm-up
→ Overall Day 15: First campaign email
→ Overall Day 38: Last new prospects added
→ Overall Day 61: Final follow-ups sent

The key: Plan for new emails AND follow-ups, not just new prospects.

Otherwise, your sending volume can catch you off guard.

DM me if your business is looking for its own lead generation machine. I will be happy to build you one...

Inspired by Suprava Sabat (is this math correct?), use Smartlead for good automation

#ColdEmail #LeadGeneration #B2BSales

We got 12 positive replies from just 30 cold emails.

That's a 40% positive reply rate.

And guess where we found these leads?

OUR SPAM FOLDER 🤭

People kept sending us cold emails that landed in spam.

So we decided to sell to the people trying to sell to us.

Here's what we did:

Scraped cold emails from our spam folder.

Used Prospeo.io to enrich the companies and filter for our ICP.

Vibecoded a free cold email analyser trained on 1,000+ of our best-performing cold emails.

Sent it to those prospects as a free lead magnet.

Offered a free cold email health check to diagnose why their emails were landing in spam.

The results?

30 emails sent.
12 positive replies.
40% positive reply rate.

We turned our spam folder into a lead list and a vibecoded tool into a lead magnet.

And the funniest part?

We're now pitching the people who originally tried to pitch us 😭

[POST] 2026-11-10 | activity 7525896895383322624 | likes 39 | comments 17 | shares 1
Link: https://www.linkedin.com/feed/update/urn:li:activity:7525896895383322624

Your best customers are literally telling you who to sell to next.

Yet most founders keep searching for leads from scratch.

Here's a smarter way to find your next 100 potential customers:

1. Pick your 5 best customers.

The ones who paid well, closed fast, and got results.

2. Find what they have in common.

Industry, company size, business model, and growth stage.

3. Find companies just like them.

I use Prospeo.io's Company Lookalike.

Just enter your best customer's domain and find similar companies.

Then narrow the list using 40+ filters.

4. Find the decision-makers.

Get their verified emails through Prospeo and build a targeted outreach list.

5. Reach out with proof.

Show them how you've helped businesses similar to theirs.

That's it.

Instead of guessing who might buy, find more companies like the ones that already did.

Your existing customers might be your best prospecting strategy.
